Evidence state

Partway: 194 scenes of this volume have been read and anchored so far. The rest of this volume is still being read; nothing else about it is published.

What happens, chapter by chapter

Ch. 1 · pp. 10–11Sakura Airi's Soliloquy

No scene — pure interior monologue, volume cold open.

  • First-person chapter from Sakura Airi (the only non-Ayanokōji POV chapter header in the volume's structure).
  • She confesses she cannot interact with people and "came up with a solution": adopting a false face to hide her true self.
  • Asks whether everyone else also performs a façade; concludes she'd never know because she can't connect — "Therefore, I remain alone."
  • Ends on the contradiction that drives her arc (p. 10): "I… I want to connect with someone from the bottom of my heart." then "And so I will continue to live quietly, with my eyes downcast. Alone."
Ch. 2 · pp. 12–39The Sudden Beginning of Our Tumultuous Troubles

June 30 evening (school building, post-brawl) → July 1, Class D: homeroom, lunch, walk to dorms, Ayanokōji's room at night.

  • Cold open, Sakura's POV (pp. 12–13) — Searching for a selfie spot, Sakura Airi walks on Sudou finishing a one-sided three-on-one beating; she photographs it silently, freezes, and flees before he can spot her ("If I'd lagged a mere two seconds, he probably would've seen me.", p.13). The volume's witness is seeded before the protagonist even knows the fight happened.
  • 2.1 Points day (pp. 15–18) — First class-point payout in Class D's history: 87 points vs Class A's 1004; Horikita reads the across-the-board ~100 gain as mere midterm reward; Hirata translates her cryptic "get something"; the deposit is then announced delayed for all first-years, with Chabashira's ominous "If there are still points left, that is." (p.19).
  • 2.2 Lunch (pp. 19–21) — AK's friends-but-alone paradox in full; Horikita's bento economics (free supermarket ingredients) and her surfboard roast; Kōenji and Sakura as fellow solo eaters.
  • 2.3 After school (pp. 22–26) — Chabashira drags Sudou to the faculty room; Horikita and AK walk home debating whether the point trouble is school-side or student-side; their hate-is-fine honesty about Kushida ("Kushida-san and I are like oil and water.", p.25).
  • 2.4 The crisis conference (pp. 27–38) — Sudou confesses: three Class C boys (Komiya, Kondou — his own basketball clubmates — plus Ishizaki) tried to blackmail him out of his shot at summer-tournament regular; he beat them, they reported first, and he faces a summer suspension plus class-wide point loss unless he produces proof by Tuesday. He felt someone watching (p.34) — the witness problem is live. Kushida drives the plan (get confessions or find the eyewitness), volunteers her friendship with Komiya's group, and pins a dodging AK to participation. AK's balance: 8,320 points (p.27).
  • Chapter close (p.38) — "No matter how much I thought about it, I couldn't come up with any solutions."
  • New named entities — Komiya, Kondou, Ishizaki (Class C, basketball), Hondou (Class D, mentioned).
Ch. 3 · pp. 40–65Weak Point

July 2, one school day — homeroom, the cafeteria at lunch, then after school in Class D's classroom, the hallway and the entrance, and the doorway of Class B's classroom.

  • Homeroom (pp.40–43) — Chabashira announces the Sudou–Class C fight to the whole class, in her "serene" neutrality, and puts out the school's terms: judgment next Tuesday, "taking into account any eyewitnesses and evidence" (p.41), and every teacher will brief their own class, so the affair becomes school-wide property (p.41). She asks for witnesses; nobody raises a hand — including the one person in the room who saw it. Ike's objection is arithmetic ("will we be at zero again this month?", p.41), Kushida then re-tells Sudou's whole account to the room (p.42), Yamauchi's one-sentence doubt about Sudou's junior-high bragging breaks her appeal (p.42–43), and only Hirata standing ("I want to believe him.") with Karuizawa after him turns the class into an investigation team (p.43).
  • 3.1 The cafeteria (pp.44–53) — Horikita refuses flatly — "I've never once considered you a friend. Nothing makes me more uncomfortable than being around someone who doesn't recognize his own stupidity." (p.45) — and the group cannot work out why, because they read her refusal as personal dislike. Kushida puts the question to AK (p.46) and he answers with a guess ("Horikita is smart, right? So I think she probably had some idea.", p.47), while privately holding her read: the school had already told the teachers enough for her to see "the possibility of a happy ending was almost nonexistent" (p.46). The rest of the scene is the boys' romance council — Ike's phone-tracking of Kushida (p.50), the "7 outta 10" ranking talk (p.51), Sudou's Horikita fantasy (p.51), and the girlfriend contest with its "Envy Tax" (p.53) — over which AK keeps noting that Sudou's problem is unresolved (p.53).
  • 3.2 After school (pp.54–63) — the class splits into canvassing teams — Hirata and Karuizawa's "Hero & Gal Team", Kushida's "Beautiful Girl & Entourage Team" (p.54). Kushida drags AK out of the room by asking, chases Horikita down to the entrance, and is refused twice: "I have an important daily routine, so I need my alone time." and "Maybe the Class C students really did start this fight, but Sudou-kun was also one of the perpetrators." (pp.56–57). Horikita then hands the explanation to AK in front of her ("Even though he pretends not to understand what I'm thinking, he probably does.", p.57) and AK gives Kushida the volume's coldest lecture — image-as-evidence, the two-suspects thought experiment, "the less information you have upon which to base your judgment, the more you have to rely on whatever meager information you have at hand" (pp.59–60). Kushida accepts the logic and rejects the conclusion ("I don't agree with abandoning Sudou-kun just to punish him.", p.60), then takes AK's advice on how to help (p.60), overrides his one-by-one plan in favour of asking several people at once (p.62), and leads the group to Class B on his ranking argument (p.61).
  • 3.3 Class B (p.64–65) — the away-classroom visit — Ike and Yamauchi frozen in the doorway, Kushida at home among strangers, Class B's hair and skirts less reserved than AK expected (pp.63–64), and AK's window-side grumble "I wanna go home." answered by Ike's "I think people who participate in clubs are stupid" (p.64). The chapter closes mid-hook: "I waited for a few minutes until I received Kushida's report. I certainly didn't expect what she told me." (p.64).
  • What this chapter is about, structurally — it is the volume's pressure test on each character's stated principle — Horikita's class-rank logic against Kushida's unconditional friendship against Hirata's faith in classmates — with the actual witness sitting silent in the same building the whole time.
  • Page map note — pp.49, 58 and 65 carry no prose (illustration/section-break pages); Chapter 4 begins on p.66.
  • New named entities — none. New system facts: the school phone's registered-friend location tracking (p.50) and the deferred disciplinary judgment dated to next Tuesday (p.41).
  • Threads left open — who watched AK in the two-thirds-empty classroom (p.62); whether any Class B student saw anything (p.64); Horikita's refusal standing until Tuesday (p.41).
Ch. 4 · pp. 66–151An Unexpected Witness

the days between the July 2 announcement and the following Sunday afternoon — Class D's classroom and its hallway, the doorway of Class B, the corridor and stairwell of the special building after school, Ayanokōji's dorm room and the dorm lobby, the route to school, the spot behind the gymnasium, the tree-lined pathway, and a shopping mall with a school-licensed electronics store. Disciplinary judgment has been promised for Tuesday and does not arrive inside this chapter; the mall trip is dated by the line "On Sunday afternoon, I went to the shopping mall to meet Kushida and fulfill my promise" (p.132), and the love letter is timed for "Friday evening at 4:00 PM, behind the gymnasium" (p.123).

  • 4.0 The class-change audience (pp.66–74) — the canvassing of Class B pays out in the worst possible currency — a homeroom discussion of whether a student can buy their way into a better class. The volume's point economics are dumped on the class in one sitting: 20,000,000 points as the price of a class change, a private-point ceiling of 3.6–4 million, the story of a twelve-million-point fraudster who was expelled, club and philanthropic activity as sources of class points, and the school's test-point shop (pp.68–72). Chabashira glances at the two students who have actually bought a test point (p.68); the narrator's verdict is that "our homeroom teacher was being extremely and deliberately mean" (p.71). Ayanokōji moves Horikita not with feeling but with arithmetic — "Doesn’t this mean there’s some value in saving Sudou?" (p.72) — and stops the instant she concedes (p.73).
  • 4.1 Horikita names the witness (pp.75–82) — while the class still believes it has no lead, Horikita produces one from an observation nobody else logged — when Kushida announced she was looking for a witness, Sakura looked down (p.77). The room cannot place Sakura; Sudō misnames her, Yamauchi denies ever having confessed to her and then retracts the denial in the same breath (p.79–82), and the narrator admits he "could barely match Sakura’s name to a face" (p.82). Ike is headlocked for describing her body out loud. The chapter's plan is set by the one person who can reach her: "Well, I’ll try to talk to her myself tomorrow," Kushida says (p.82).
  • 4.2–4.3 The approach fails in public (pp.83–96) — Hirata agrees to greet Sakura but refuses to be the one who pressures her (p.83–84), Karuizawa is ruled out in advance as too blunt for her (p.83), and Kushida's attempt collapses in front of the whole class — the camera is knocked over, Sakura apologises and flees (p.86–90). Ayanokōji alone reads it as a flight response, citing cultural anthropologist Edward Hall's intimate space (p.88), and notices she concealed her dominant hand (p.88). Kushida then asks him publicly whether he is Sudō's ally (p.91); Horikita writes Sudō off aloud, the class's last organised defence of him dies in an argument with Kōenji, a desk is kicked apart, and Hirata physically stops him (p.92–93). Ike gives up on the case at p.91; the narrator discovers the classroom's hidden cameras at p.95, and Horikita asks him to walk back with her at p.96.
  • 4.4 The special building, and Class B's offer (pp.97–107) — at the scene of the fight the narrator tells Horikita "Sudou was the first friend that I made here. I want to help him." (p.97) and privately states the case's actual state — "Our only proof was Sudou’s word. We were completely in the dark." (p.100). Sakura passes them and neither of them can make her testify; he lets her go (p.102). Ichinose Honami of Class B arrives, hears the whole account from a boy she barely knows (p.104), and offers help on the ground that "If the party that lied wins, that’d set a bad precedent" (p.106). Horikita decides, not him: "I didn’t really have the power to decide in the first place; that was Horikita’s job." (p.106). The information gap becomes his stated grievance against the school (p.107).
  • 4.5–4.6 Horikita in his room, then the dorm lobby (pp.108–115) — she follows him home, calls coming there "a shameful act in itself. A disgrace." (p.111), checks his room for cameras, and delivers the volume's most dangerous read of him — "You’re hiding in plain sight. You disgust me more than anyone else." (p.112). In the dorm lobby the next morning, Ichinose greets him by name: the contact exchange (p.109), the discovery that Class B invented its own offices (class representative, vice representative, secretary) while Class D has none (p.114–115), and the report that Class A's homeroom teacher gets a special bonus (p.108).
  • 4.6–4.7 Walking to school, and the confession behind the gymnasium (pp.116–127) — Ichinose and the narrator walk to school, and the chapter stops being about Sudō for eleven pages. She volunteers Class B's figures ("We have about 600 points, so our classes are around 350 points apart.", p.117), expects a hidden summer task bigger than either exam (p.117), and argues out loud that the four classes were not sorted by ability alone — "I think that good students and good athletes were placed into Class D for a reason, as a countermeasure." (p.120). Then she asks whether a girl has ever confessed to him (p.121), and after class makes him wait behind the gymnasium as a fake boyfriend so a Class B girl named Chihiro will be hurt less by rejection (p.124). He refuses to be the lie: "I’m just a friend." (p.126), lectures both of them on what a confession deserves, walks away without waiting for the answer, and she goes back and tells the truth anyway (p.127).
  • 4.8 Kushida's phone call (pp.128–131) — she calls him late, first of her own accord, to take responsibility for Sakura's broken camera and to borrow him for the repair errand (p.128–129); she carries Horikita's conclusion — "Horikita-san seems convinced that Sakura saw everything." (p.129) — and the class's whole estimate of itself: "Class D was able to come together, thanks to you and Hirata." (p.130). He asks whether her room is on the ninth floor (p.130) and the call dies into a silence she starts three sentences inside and finishes with nothing: "No, it’s nothing." (p.131).
  • 4.9–4.10 The mall, the repair counter, the witness (pp.132–146) — she tests whether he has been excluded from his own friend group — "I’m talking about Ike-kun and the others going to that café, of course." (p.133) — then Sakura appears in a hat and a surgical mask, already sitting unnoticed on the same bench (p.136). At the counter Sakura freezes in "blatant fear and revulsion" (p.138); the clerk's interest is tolerated, and the narrator sees what the two girls do not: "the clerk had unsettling eyes" (p.140). He takes the pen, writes his own name and dorm room on the repair form, and daring the clerk to object (p.140) — the repair is quoted at two weeks (p.140). He then phones Sotomura, "the Professor", from the shop floor about a device he never names, priced against a laptop another student bought for 80,000 points, and books the trip as a "preliminary inspection" because he does not have enough points to buy anything (p.143–144). Kushida asks whether they have met before, asks to see Sakura without her glasses, is refused, and lets it go (p.144). She then declines the thanks, asks Sakura to stop speaking so formally, and is answered by a girl who agrees to try (p.145). The chapter's turn arrives as a gift offered for a day of errands: "I-I may be able to help you with Sudou-kun’s case." — "In her own words, Sakura told us that she was the witness." (p.146).
  • 4.11 Three calls, none of them finished (pp.147–151) — that night the narrator and Kushida reconstruct Sakura's armour — the fake glasses, deduced from the absence of lens distortion, and his conclusion that "she probably wears them because she doesn’t want others to see her true self." (p.148). Sakura calls him instead of her (p.148–149), says thank you, asks "What were you…thinking about?" in a question nobody can answer, and hangs up with "I’m sorry, it’s nothing. Goodnight." (p.149). Kushida confirms she heard nothing. The volume's last page of Chapter 4 is the narrator admitting that his puzzle pieces are "too small, and too few", and deciding he cannot approach Sakura without making her stand out (p.150).
  • What this chapter is about, structurally — the case is solved by everyone except the people whose job it was — Horikita is right about the witness and cannot reach her, Kushida reaches her but cannot extract the testimony in a classroom, Hirata refuses to ask at all, and the two students nobody credits (the narrator, and a girl the class cannot picture) close it in a shopping mall. The chapter's second half then refuses to be about Sudō at all: it spends eleven pages on a confession Class D never hears about, and ends on a witness who has promised to testify and a boy who cannot work out why she telephoned him.
  • Page map note — pp.87, 134 and 151 carry no prose (illustration/section-break pages); p.113 carries only its closing two lines. Chapter 5 begins on p.152.
  • New named entities — Ichinose Honami is formally named and given her first name (p.109); Hoshinomiya Chie, Class B's homeroom teacher, is described through Ichinose (p.108); Chihiro, an unsorted Class B girl who confesses to Ichinose (p.125–127); Sotomura, "the Professor", is called by name and re-enters as the volume's technical expert (p.143); Yukimura is named once, in the narrator's list of Class D's misfits (p.119); Higashiyama-sensei is remembered as the P.E. instructor who promised 5000 points for the best time in their first pool class (p.71); the school-licensed mall stores and their clerk enter as locations and a minor antagonist (p.138–141).
  • New system facts — the class-change price and its ceiling, the twelve-million-point expulsion, club and philanthropic activity as class-point sources, the test-point shop and its verification (p.68–72); classroom cameras as the grading instrument, with none in the hallways (p.95); the homeroom-teacher bonus rumour (p.108); the self-made Class B offices against Class D's absence of them (p.114–115); the school-linked electronics store, its warranty and repair turnaround (p.138–140); and the first hard consumer price a student states, 80,000 points for a laptop (p.143).
  • Threads left open — Tuesday's judgment, still not delivered (p.41, carried forward); what Sakura wanted to say on the phone (p.149); whether Kushida's interrupted sentence and the ninth-floor question are connected (p.130–131); who Chihiro is to Class B's balance of power (p.127); the unnamed device being price-checked at the mall (p.143–144); Ike's café gathering that the narrator was not told about (p.133); and the camera itself, out for a two-week repair that this chapter does not wait for (p.140).
  • Attribution gaps in the translation, recorded rather than guessed — an untagged modest reply in the p.129–130 phone exchange ("I’m just doing what I normally do. It wasn’t anything special.") is not assigned to either speaker; the question about whether Sakura photographs people (p.142) is untagged; the first exchange of the p.147 night call is untagged on both sides; the reply "You kind of look suspicious…" (p.136) sits between two named speakers with no tag of its own; and in the p.48–53 cafeteria scene and the p.90–96 classroom exchange, several lines have no speaker named and are absent from these records rather than attributed.
Ch. 5 · pp. 152–189Each and Every Prediction

the day before the deliberation and the night of it — the school entrance and its bulletin board, Class D's classroom, the faculty room, Ayanokōji's dorm room, Sakura's room on the eleventh floor, the club-activities building, and the dorm's shared spaces. The chapter ends the night before Tuesday's judgment; the hearing itself is not shown.

  • 5.0 A poster, a message board, a leak (pp.152–158) — with one day left, the class is united but lacks "firm, irrefutable evidence" (p.152). Two canvasses run in parallel: Kanzaki of Class B pinned a bounty flyer to the bulletin board (p.153), and Ichinose posted to the school homepage's message board (p.153). Both offer points, and the money is Class B's own (p.154). Two replies arrive, and one of them re-frames the fight: Ishizaki was a junior-high brawler, which makes a three-on-one loss suspect rather than heroic (p.156). Kanzaki's reading — that the three let Sudō beat them as a trap — is the chapter's first real theory (p.156). Ayanokōji keeps Sakura's name out of the conversation (p.156), teaches Ichinose how to pay an anonymous informant with a temporary token key (pp.157–158), and catches one unreadable glimpse of her phone that he files away as a future problem (p.158).
  • 5.1 The face Horikita does not intend to make (pp.159–162) — Kushida's morning thanks, and Horikita's reaction to it, are the chapter's quiet centre — she denies making any expression while the page says otherwise (pp.159–160), and Kushida reads the same face as exclusion rather than anger (p.162).
  • 5.2 The witness is tested (pp.163–166) — in the faculty room, Sakura gives the account in full for the first time (p.163). Chabashira does not welcome it: she asks why the testimony is late, then names the class's motive out loud — "it may be a lie made up by Class D" (p.164) — before accepting it anyway and setting the terms: Sakura must attend, Sudō will be told, and only two classmates may sit in (p.165). Horikita claims both seats over Kushida (p.165).
  • 5.3 The strategy lunch (pp.167–168) — Sudō's confidence, Horikita's refusal to reassure him, and Ike and Yamauchi fighting over a manga magazine in the middle of it (p.167). Kushida notices something she will not name yet and reaches for her phone (p.168).
  • 5.4 The room on the eleventh floor (pp.169–175) — Sakura asks him to come alone, and he rides up past the floor he claimed not to be visiting while Horikita, shopping bags in hand, asks him the question and then withdraws it (pp.170–171). In her room Sakura says she does not have the confidence to testify and considers staying home (p.171). The night's real content is her explanation of why she can talk to him — not his eyes but "the pupils, way back in the eyes" (p.172) — and his offer of friendship, which she accepts as if it were a surprise (p.173). Her glasses fall off, and the page confirms what the class had already guessed: there are no lenses (p.174).
  • 5.5 Advice in a club building (pp.176–178) — she takes him to the club-activities building, and the advice he gives her is deliberately not encouragement: testify for herself, not for Sudō or Kushida or the class (p.177). Whether it helps is left open (p.178).
  • 5.6 What the class finds (pp.179–185) — Kushida identifies Sakura as the gravure idol Shizuku from a blog, and the proof is mundane — a dormitory door in one photograph (p.180). Her theory of the double life is that the idol face is the construction, not the disguise (p.183). Then, at the door, she makes her deferred request explicit — help her, and she will give him "my most precious possession" (p.184) — and the narrator's judgment of her hardens (pp.184–185).
  • 5.7 The comments (pp.186–188) — that night he reads the blog's history and finds a single commenter escalating for months, including a post from the previous Sunday (pp.186–187). The connection he makes is the electronics-store clerk who took her repair form (p.187). The chapter ends with nothing actionable: the only option is to wait for her to ask for help (p.188).
  • What this chapter is about, structurally — the witness found in Chapter 4 becomes a person with a second life in Chapter 5, and every institution around her — teacher, class, school message board — handles her evidence while missing her. The chapter's two most consequential discoveries, the lensless glasses and the stalker, are made by people she did not tell.
  • Page map note — pp.155, 161, 182 and 189 carry no prose (illustration/section-break pages); pp.154, 160, 166, 178 and 188 carry only a short closing passage. Chapter 6 begins on p.190.
  • New named entities — Kanzaki Ryūji of Class B, introduced by Ichinose, who pinned the bounty flyer (p.153); Shizuku, the gravure-idol name Sakura worked under (p.179); Ishizaki gains a junior-high history as a fighter (p.156).
  • New system facts — the school homepage has a student-visible message board (p.153); points can be sent anonymously via a temporary token key generated from the payer's ID number (pp.157–158); entry to the girls' floor of the dormitory is allowed but barred after 8:00 PM (pp.169–170); a deliberation admits at most two observing classmates (p.165).
  • Threads left open — what Ayanokōji saw on Ichinose's phone (p.158); the request Kushida will make once the case is settled (p.184); what Kushida noticed at lunch (p.168); the stalker, still unidentified and still on campus (p.187); Tuesday's judgment, promised since p.41 and still not delivered (p.165).
  • Attribution gaps in the translation, recorded rather than guessed — the manga-magazine exchange at p.167 leaves the second speaker untagged; the blog reveal at p.179 opens with two untagged lines ("Wait, is that a picture of Shizuku?" and "Shizuku?") before any speaker is named.
Ch. 6 · pp. 190–219Truth and Lies

the day of the disciplinary hearing — Class D's classroom and the faculty room before 4:00 PM, the student council room on the fourth floor for the hearing itself, then the corridor outside it and the entranceway afterwards. The retrial is set for 4:00 PM the following day, outside this chapter.

  • 6.0 The morning she comes anyway (p.190) — Sakura is in the classroom early, gloomier than usual and sitting alone; she says she came because the class would be upset if she were absent. Ayanokōji repeats the club-building instruction — "Testify for your own sake, more than anyone else's." Ike and Yamauchi now know about the idol work; Sakura notices and mouths "It's all right."
  • 6.1 The hearing (pp.191–209) — the room is the student council's, with Secretary Tachibana running the bulk of it, President Horikita Manabu at the back, both homeroom teachers, and the three Class C students. Horikita freezes when she learns the council will sit in and has to be physically roused when Sakura's testimony stalls (pp.192–197). Sakura fails to speak for twenty seconds, is ordered out, and then produces the volume's turning evidence from the doorway: "I definitely saw what happened!" (pp.199–200). Her photographs establish that she was in the special building, and the president finds the one that shows the fight itself (pp.203–204). Sakagami cannot undo the photo, so he proposes a settlement — two weeks' suspension for Sudō, one for his students — and Chabashira accepts it as "a logical conclusion" (pp.205–206). Ayanokōji performs surrender to force Horikita back into the argument; she delivers a damning closing statement, refuses to sit, and states that Class D will not accept even one day of suspension (pp.206–208). The president sets a retrial for the next afternoon and warns that expulsion is on the table (p.209).
  • 6.2 After the hearing (pp.209–218) — Sakura breaks down in the corridor over Sakagami's parting speech; Horikita promises to keep fighting and leaves (pp.209–210). The president stays to tell Ayanokōji and Sakura that Class D evidence can never be accepted at full value, then concedes the conversation is the closest he has come to one (pp.210–212). On the way out Sakura recovers enough to thank Ayanokōji, starts a sentence at the entranceway, and loses it when Ichinose and Kanzaki appear (pp.213–214). Class B argues the case is already lost and then offers to keep searching; Horikita asks them for something instead — a preparation that requires a lie — and Ichinose lends her phone (pp.214–218). The chapter ends with Horikita collecting the cost of the tickling in the hearing: a blow to the side and a promise to pay it back double (p.218).
  • What this chapter is about, structurally — the volume's two engines meet for the first time. The disciplinary case is decided procedurally — the evidence is accepted, then discounted by class; the compromise is offered, then refused on principle; and the retrial is granted because neither side will call itself a liar. The chapter's real turn is not the photo but Horikita's refusal to sit, and the real cost is paid by Sakura, who wins the room and then has to walk out through Sakagami's sentence.
  • Page map note — p.201 and p.219 carry no prose (illustration/section-break pages); p.210 opens with the tail of the corridor scene. Chapter 7 begins on p.220.
  • New named entities — Sakagami-sensei, Class C's homeroom teacher (p.193); Secretary Tachibana, the student council secretary (p.193, surname only); Horikita Manabu is named in narration for the first time in this volume (p.208); the Class C trio is fixed by name here — Komiya, Kondou and Ishizaki (pp.194–198).
  • New system facts — a disciplinary settlement is heard by the two homeroom teachers, the concerned students and the student council rather than the faculty room (p.192); the student council president can take over a case and the secretary handles the rest (p.193); a retrial can be set for the next day, with expulsion as a possible judgment if neither side is proven to have lied (p.209); extensions of the retrial window are not granted on request (p.209); a proposed settlement can assign different suspension lengths by degree of injury (p.205).
  • Threads left open — the retrial at 4:00 PM the next day and its outcome (p.209); the plan Horikita asks Class B to prepare, and the phone she borrows (pp.216–218); the Professor's part in it (p.217); what Sakura began to say at the entranceway (pp.213–214); Kanzaki's warning that the favor may come back against Class B (p.217); and the stalker thread from Chapter 5, untouched here.
  • Attribution gaps in the translation, recorded rather than guessed — the opening courtesy in the student council room — "It's before the scheduled start time. There's no need for apologies." (p.193) — has no speaker tag; "If Class D has no further proof to offer, would you mind if we continued the proceedings?" (p.196) is untagged; the witness introduction "1-D, Sakura Airi-san." and "You may begin your testimony, if you wouldn't mind, Sakura-san." (p.199) are untagged; "What are those?" (p.203) is untagged; "I think that you'll believe I was there after seeing…this." (p.204) is untagged; Class D's answer "I haven't lied. Everything we've said has been the truth." (p.209) is untagged; and "Mmmhmm, I see. An eye for an eye, a lie for a lie, yeah?…" (p.217) is untagged.
  • Translation defects kept as printed — "This are inadequate evidence" (p.204); "Sakagamisensei's expression stiffened" as a run-together name (p.203–204); "Would be possible to have a little more time before we reconvene?" with the subject dropped (p.209); "Yahoo!" left as printed in both teachers' greetings (pp.191, 214).
Ch. 7 · pp. 220–257Only One Solution

the last day of the Sudō case — the tree-lined path to school and the classroom in the morning, the special building after 3:40, the shuttered front of an electronics store, then the student council room for the retrial and the corridor outside it. Ayanokōji narrates through §7.4; §7.5 is Horikita’s first-person section, the volume’s second carried by a different narrator after Sakura’s opening; §7.6 returns to Ayanokōji.

  • 7.0 The morning of the verdict (pp.220–221) — Horikita waits for him on the path and calls the day what it is — "The truth is that we’re gambling" — while taking the responsibility for Sudō onto herself. She begins "After we settle this case," and swallows the rest.
  • 7.1 Sakura stands up straight (pp.222–223) — Sakura is early, sits "as if ready for something," and gives her first morning greeting; Horikita measures the change coldly — people do not change easily, and overreaching is how Sakura would fail.
  • 7.2 The trap in the corridor (pp.224–233) — Sakura, wired before the trial, sends Ayanokōji off with "if there’s something I need to move forward… I’ll do it" and puts their talk off until tomorrow. At 3:40 he has lured Ishizaki’s trio to the special building on an email he asked Kushida to send; Ichinose arrives and applies the pressure — the school’s strange delay, the cameras, the school’s own test of them — while Komiya buckles and Kondou breaks. Ishizaki refuses the trade and offers himself as "an honorable sacrifice," then reaches to call "that person" and is denied the time; faced with expulsion, he kneels and agrees to withdraw. The four of them walk the three C students up to the student council room — "Horikita had put everything together really well."
  • 7.3 The cameras were never the school’s (pp.234–235) — the reveal — Ichinose and Kanzaki bought the cameras and hung them with the Professor at lunch; the heat and the standoff did the rest. She calls him a future rival, promises the borrowed points back by graduation, and starts to say one more thing, but he is already running after Sakura, Ichinose following.
  • 7.4 The shuttered door (pp.236–241) — Sakura is cornered by her fan — the electronics-store clerk who has written to her room — and Ayanokōji and Ichinose break it up as a pair of delinquents with cameras; the clerk runs. In the quiet after, Sakura gives a slight nod and Ayanokōji tells Ichinose the idol name, Shizuku; the new footing is set — she can talk to him, and should consult Horikita and Kushida too. Then Ichinose delivers what she had been holding: someone was pulling the strings behind the whole case, and his name is Ryuuen.
  • 7.5 The retrial (pp.242–251) — from Horikita’s seat — her brother, the frozen throat, Chabashira noticing Ayanokōji’s absence — and before the room can sit, the three Class C students withdraw their complaint on the spot; there is no verdict, only a point payment for the deliberation expenses nobody had heard of, and a clearing of both sides. Chabashira keeps Sudō for the lecture he has earned — "There’s strength in admitting your mistakes, Sudou" — and he answers with his first penitent words. Then she sends the room away and keeps Horikita: Class D is an assemblage of "defective products," Ayanokōji is in her personal opinion the most defective of them, and the three questions he must be studied through — the fifties on his entrance exam, his help, his refusal to stand forward — end in "Get to know the person called Ayanokouji."
  • 7.6 The corridor (pp.252–257) — outside, the president thanks Ayanokōji and offers him the secretary seat on the spot; he refuses it without interest. Horikita catches him with the puppet question and walks his own orchestration back at him; he answers with "the first friends I’ve ever made" and keeps walking. A black-haired boy steps into the path — "Next time, I’ll be your opponent. I look forward to it." — and Ayanokōji’s closing inventory files the encounter as a declaration of war from Ryuuen, finds Class A in three years hopeless against him and Ichinose, and turns on himself: "I was a horrible human being."
  • What this chapter is about, structurally — the case is solved offstage by a bluff and ratified onstage without a fight; the point of view changes so the victory never has to be watched, and the corridor afterwards converts it into the volume’s true trade — a declaration of war in exchange for a win Ayanokōji did not want to be part of. The "only one solution" of the title is Ichinose’s: make the incident not exist.
  • Page map note — §7.5 opens on p.242 with the point-of-view change; p.257 carries no prose; Chapter 8, the Postscript, begins on p.258.
  • New named entities — Ryuuen Kakeru, named for the first time in this volume (pp.241, 255), on page only in the corridor scene that withholds his name (p.254); the electronics-store clerk, Sakura’s fan, is never named.
  • New system facts — a withdrawn case closes with no verdict and no winners or losers, and still costs the withdrawing side a point payment for deliberation expenses, announced in the room with no speaker attached (p.245); the school sells surveillance equipment as study tools, and the only cameras in the building sit by the faculty room and the science lab (pp.229, 234); the student council president can appoint a secretary on the spot, with one earlier first-year applicant already rejected after the first interview (pp.252–253); hearing witnesses are to be rewarded, the form of the reward left unstated (p.246).
  • Threads left open — Ishizaki’s unheard telephone call to "that person" (p.232) — the later pages name Ryuuen as the puller but never show the call; Ryuuen’s declared next opposition (p.254); Horikita’s puppet charge and Ayanokōji’s evasion (pp.253–255); Ayanokōji’s suspicion that Chabashira may know his past (p.255); the borrowed points due back by graduation (p.235); Ichinose’s standing offer of help (p.241).
  • Attribution gaps in the translation, recorded rather than guessed — on the morning path two lines have no speaker — "Would you be happy if we’d compromised?" and, after Horikita takes the responsibility onto herself, "So you do worry about things like this, huh?" (p.220); "What’s going on? Why are you here?" precedes Ishizaki stepping forward and carries no tag (p.225); "You can’t trick us like that. You guys installed that camera yourselves!" is fixed only to "his two accomplices ... as they answered," without choosing between Komiya and Kondou (p.229); "H-hey, Ishizaki. It’s not too late to tell them we lied! If we do, the school might forgive us!" has no speaker (p.231–232); "G-good morning…" answers Sakura’s first greeting with no tag, and so does the answer to Horikita’s "What’s with her?" — "Maybe because of what happened yesterday, she took a step forward on the path to adulthood?" (p.222); the delinquent-act exchange runs untagged — "Ooh, ‘Adult harasses high school girl.’ I can just see the headlines tomorrow; it’ll be a huge scandal!", "Hmm, it certainly doesn’t sound wrong. Does it? It kind of looks that way, don’t you think?", and "Wrong? I don’t think so. Whoa, look at all those letters! Gross. Are you a stalker?" (p.237); at p.234 the lend-points line fixes Ayanokōji as the lender, but "I couldn’t believe you wanted to install security cameras." has no tag; "Sorry. For being quiet for so long." and its reply run without tags (p.240); the point-fee regulations line is spoken in the president’s seat-time with no speaker attached (p.245).
  • Translation defects kept as printed — "There was no truly major difference between the today’s Sakura and yesterday’s" (p.222); "I have no intension of arguing" (p.226); "Ishizaki and the other seemed to be at a loss for words" (p.229); "if the security footage were brought produced" (p.232); "in order to cover of miscellaneous expenses" (p.245); "as if he just shaken off something heavy" (p.248); quotation marks left open at "Who came up with the strategy that defeated Class C?" (p.248) and "Am I just your puppet?" (p.253).
Ch. 8 · pp. 258–261Postscript

outside the story. The author’s afterword, printed after Chapter 7, in which Syougo Kinugasa writes to the reader for the first time in this volume; four months have passed since the last book. No page of it is in-world evidence — no character fact, point total or school rule appears here.

  • What he says the book was (p.258) — "Anyway, this book focuses on a disturbance centered on Sudou, following the midterms." He allows that "since Class D is filled with all kinds of problem children, the day where they can work together is still a long ways off", calls the volume an opening act — "You could say that the first act was a fierce battle waged over class points" — and credits it with showing the cast from an angle the reader did not have: "I think that the classmates also revealed things about themselves that weren’t visible before." The promise attached is "the story will progress greatly from this point on."
  • The illustrator, and the cover (p.258) — "Shunsaku-sama drew such gorgeous illustrations for this book. I nearly suffocated when I saw the cover art with Kushida. It just had the best expression." The standing joke between them is reported, and it is about this series’ cast: "can you please stop clicking your tongue at me whenever new male characters show up in the story?" The yakiniku promised in the first volume was paid for, at the wrong price — "I’d originally planned to take you to an all-you-can-eat place that cost 1280 Yen, but after some pestering, I ended up paying about 3980 Yen for one order of some high-class yakiniku" — and the return invitation is open: "I’d like to eat some sashimi. Maybe some maguro, or some maguro, or some maguro." Whether the illustrator ever treats him is deferred to the next book: "I will report in the following volume whether or not I was able to successfully make Shunsaku-sama treat me."
  • Acknowledgements (p.259) — his editor, "thank you very much for staying on top of me until I met my deadline", and the readers — "Even during times when my hands won’t move because of (what is likely) my poor physical health, the fact that people are holding this book in their hands and reading it gives me the encouragement to keep going."
  • His health, as the afterword keeps returning to it (pp.258, 260) — "Lately I’ve been suffering every day from some dull pain on my right side, back pain, and dizziness that comes from the headaches I’ve been getting." He intends "a thorough medical examination as soon as possible", then adds "Doing physical examinations is impossible, though. I’m an old man." The biography printed with it says the same thing more bluntly: "His body has begun to break down, which has strengthened his resolve to go in for a medical examination."
  • Bios as printed (p.260) — the author is Syougo Kinugasa, "Born in November. Blood type, AB", "Primarily responsible for the scenario and PC game planning", with Guards of Daybreak and Reminiscence listed as his most important works. The illustrator is Tomoseshunsaku, "Born in September. The lead animator and illustrator for the Sagittarius games", lead animator for the same two titles, and his entry ends on a wager: "If he loses eight kilograms by the end of the year, his friends will buy him a PlayStation 4, which has strengthened his resolve to work hard to lose weight."
  • Page map note — p.261 carries only the publisher’s newsletter notice, no prose of the book’s own. The afterword closes Year 1 Volume 2; the narrative ended at p.257.
  • Translation defects kept as printed — "the day where they can work together is still a long ways off" (p.258); "Doing physical examinations is impossible, though." (p.258); the illustrator is addressed as "Shunsaku-sama" in the afterword and printed as "Tomoseshunsaku" in the biography (pp.258, 260).

    Ike's grudging "At least him being there is better than not, I guess? Probably?" — AK's utility officially unexplained even to his defenders. — Ike Kanji p.45

  • Relationship

    Publicly defends her reasoning to the boys; Ike reads it as romance-leaning ("She’s important to him, right?"). — Horikita Suzune p.46–47

When I heard he was being considered for a regular spot on the basketball team, I was both surprised and impressed. No one disagrees that he’s an incredible player, but if he acts so arrogant and prideful, some people will come to hate him. Those who work incredibly hard for their spot would probably see Sudou as a rather disagreeable person. Then there are the rumors, right? I haven’t met anyone who knew Sudou at his previous school, but considering how many people are talking about it, there might be something to the rumor." p.59

    "This was bound to happen eventually. That’s why Horikita said Sudou was a perpetrator." p.59

  • Spoken

    "Yeah. As long as he continues to antagonize those around him, trouble will inevitably follow. Also, if there’s no proof, then people will use his image against him. In other words, they’ll judge him based on their impressions. For example, let’s say there was a murder case. There are two suspects. One of them has committed murder in the past, while the other is a good, upstanding citizen. Based on that information, who would you believe?" p.59

  • Spoken

    "The truth might be different. However, the less information you have upon which to base your judgment, the more you have to rely on whatever meager information you have at hand. That’s what’s happening here. Horikita cannot overlook the fact that Sudou doesn’t recognize his flaws." p.59

Ch.6 §6.1: the hearing he cannot fixp.191–200
8 entries · 1 quoted
  • Scene

    Class D’s room before the 4:00 PM hearing; he watches Sudō and Horikita spar and reads Sudō’s cooperation as proof he does not hate her. p.191

  • Caveat

    His estimate of Sakura is the chapter’s first alarm: "If she was in this psychological state before the meeting had even begun, she might not be able to speak satisfactorily." p.191

  • Scene

    The student council room: three Class C students, Sakagami-sensei, the president and Secretary Tachibana; Horikita freezes at the word "student council." p.193

  • Caveat

    His read of the room before a word of evidence: "Considering they were brother and sister, the odds were not in our favor." p.194

  • Caveat

    He reads Class C’s testimony as invention — the veins on Sudō’s forehead make their account "rather obvious" as a lie — and Horikita’s silence as the real emergency: "It was as if her mind was gone." p.195

  • Spoken

    "Get a grip on yourself, Horikita. We’re going to lose at this rate. You have to fight!" p.197

  • What happens

    He gets up, stands behind Horikita, grips her sides "as hard as I could" and tickles her until she returns to herself, in front of the teachers and the student council. p.196–197

  • What happens

    The provocation is deliberate and public: he accepts that he "might be killed later for doing this" but judged that no lesser stimulus would bring her back. p.196–197

Ch.6 §6.1: the performance of giving upp.202–206
7 entries · 4 quoted
  • Caveat

  • Spoken

    "Horikita, are we really out of options?" p.206

  • Spoken

    "I’m not very smart, so I can’t really come up with a solution. I do, however, think that we probably should accept the compromise that you have offered us, Sakagami-sensei," p.206

  • Spoken

    "We have no definitive proof of Sudou’s innocence. I suppose I should say such evidence just doesn’t exist. If this event had occurred in a classroom or the convenience store, a greater number of students would have been around to see it, and there probably would have been sound evidence. There’s no record of anyone watching this scene unfold. Since this event took place in the special building, where there weren’t any people around, there’s nothing we can do." p.206

  • Caveat

    The move depends on Sakagami taking it literally, which he does: "In other words, as a declaration of defeat." p.206

Chabashira Sae

Ch.2 §2.1 (July 1): the point board, and a deposit with a riderp.16–18
11 entries · 6 quoted
  • Scene

    She "strode into the classroom as the homeroom bell rang" to a Class D more raucous than usual, the first payout day since enrollment. p.16

  • Spoken

    “Good morning, everyone. You all seem more restless than usual today.” p.16

  • Spoken

    “Oh, so that’s why you’re all so restless?” p.16

  • Spoken

    “Don’t jump to conclusions. Listen to what I have to say first. You’re correct, Ike. You have all worked harder than ever before. I recognize that. Naturally, the school understands full well how you all feel.” p.16–17

  • Scene

    Puts a paper listing the month's point totals on the board, Class A at the top, rather than letting the class read the app's numbers unexplained. p.17

  • Spoken

    “Well then. Without further ado, here are this month’s point totals.” p.17

  • Spoken

    “Well, this time there was a little trouble. The first-year students’ point distribution has been delayed. I’m sorry, but you’ll have to wait a bit longer,” p.18

  • Spoken

    “Don’t blame me. This was the school’s decision, there’s nothing I can do about it. Once the trouble has been resolved, you’ll receive your points. If there are still points left, that is.” p.18

  • Trait

    The same method as entrance day, now in maintenance mode: buy the room's acknowledgment first ("You’re correct, Ike"), then deliver the bad news, then assign it to the school and refuse responsibility — closing on a conditional no student asks her to unpack. p.16–18

  • Backstory

    Ayanokōji is the only one who hears the rider as a warning: "There was deeper meaning behind Chabashira-sensei’s words." p.18

  • Backstory

    AK's memory of her entrance-day line is what taught the class to spend: he "understood that our homeroom teacher, Chabashira-sensei, hadn’t been kidding when she told us, 'At this school, you can buy anything with your points.'" p.16

Ch.2 §2.3: collecting Sudou for the faculty roomp.22
5 entries · 3 quoted
  • Spoken

    “Sudou. I have to talk to you about something. Come to the faculty room,” p.22

  • Spoken

    “I’ve already spoken with the advisor. You don’t have to come with me if you don’t want to, but you will face the consequences later.” p.22

  • Spoken

    “That all depends on you. The longer you stand there, the more time you waste.” p.22

  • Trait

    She removes the excuse before she makes the request — the club advisor has already been told, so his "I’ve got basketball practice now" buys nothing. Refuses to estimate how long it will take ("That all depends on you"). p.22

  • Relationship

    First direct contact since she cancelled his expulsion at the midterm: private summons, no audience in front of the class, and he "clicked his tongue, and followed" her out. — Sudō Ken p.22

Ch.3 open (July 2 homeroom): she makes Sudou's fight publicp.40–41
13 entries · 7 quoted
  • Scene

    About to leave homeroom, she "assaulted us with one of her infamously brief and off-hand announcements", naming Sudou and Class C in front of the whole class. p.40

  • Spoken

    “I have an announcement for you all. There was a bit of trouble the other day, an incident between the student sitting there, Sudou, and some students from Class C. In short, there was a fight.” p.40

  • Trait

    AK's read of the delivery: "Her face was so devoid of emotion or interest when she spoke that there was a certain serene beauty to it. She inserted no personal bias whatsoever when she addressed the class, and explained the situation from a position of neutrality." p.40

  • Spoken

    “The complaint came from Class C. They claim that the fight was one-sided. However, when we spoke to the accused, Sudou said that their claims were false. He insists that the students Class C called him over and started the fight.” p.40

  • Spoken

    “So in other words, we don’t yet know the truth. Therefore, we have put our decision on hold for now. Our response, and the punishment, will come when we find out who was at fault.” p.41

  • Spoken

    “So speaks the accused, but I wouldn’t say that you have a high level of credibility right now. If there is an eyewitness, as Sudou seems to believe, then the situation may change. If anyone here witnessed the fight, please raise your hand.” p.41

  • Scene

    "Chabashira-sensei continued talking in a robotic, flat voice. No students raised their hands in response to her question." p.41

  • Spoken

    “Too bad, Sudou. It appears there are no witnesses in this class.” p.41

  • Spoken

    “In order to check for witnesses, every teacher will inform their classes of the details of this incident.” p.41

  • Spoken

    “Anyway, that is all. We’ll make our final judgment next Tuesday, taking into account any eyewitnesses and evidence. With that, let’s end homeroom for the day.” p.41

  • Trait

    She destroys Sudou's containment plan as a side effect of doing her job neutrally: no editorialising, no protection, and the school-wide notification she volunteers is the step that turns a class matter into a cohort matter. AK: "The school probably had no say in the matter." p.40–41

  • Rule

    The disciplinary process stated on the page: accusation and defence both recorded, decision held pending eyewitness or evidence, judgment date fixed (next Tuesday), and every class informed so witnesses can be found outside Class D. p.41

  • Scene

    She leaves and "Sudou followed right after her" — AK's read: he stayed out of the room rather than lose his temper at his classmates. p.41

Ch.5 §5.2: the teacher who will not take the witness on trustp.163–166
16 entries · 12 quoted
  • Scene

    The faculty room, after homeroom, at the class's request; Ayanokōji hangs back. p.163

  • Spoken

    "A witness? For Sudou’s case?" p.163

  • Spoken

    "I understand what you’re said. However, I can’t simply accept what you’re telling me," p.163

  • Spoken

    "Sakura, why are you testifying now? When I reported the issue during homeroom, you didn’t step forward. It’s not as though you were absent that day, right?" p.163

  • Spoken

    "You’re not good at talking with other people, and yet you’ve decided to testify now? Isn’t that strange?" p.164

  • Spoken

    "I’m speaking to Sakura right now." p.164

  • Limit

    Her interrogation of a frightened witness reduces the girl physically: "Sakura hunched and shrank away, much like a frog cornered by a snake." The narrator adds that she "should have understood what kind of girl Sakura was" and that speaking up at all "had made great progress" — the method is exact and costly. p.164

  • Spoken

    "I see. So, then you mustered up the courage to come forward?" p.164

  • Spoken

    "I see. Well if you are a witness as you say, naturally I’m obligated to relay that information to the school. However, while the school will listen to the whole story, Sudou may not be declared innocent." p.164

  • Spoken

    "Are you really the witness, Sakura? That’s what I’m getting at. I think that it may be a lie made up by Class D because the students are afraid of receiving a negative evaluation." p.164

  • Spoken

    "Horrible? If you really had witnessed an event, you should have come forward on the first day. It’s natural to feel suspicious when someone steps up just in the nick of time. Considering that the witness is from Class D, it’s doubly suspicious. Any reasonable person would have doubts. Don’t you think so? Conveniently, a student from the same class happened to be in an infrequently visited building and happened to witness the entire event?" p.164

  • Spoken

    "However, a witness is a witness. I cannot determine if she’s lying, so for the time being, I will accept her testimony. So, Sakura, I will ask for you to join us on the day of deliberation. I understand that you do not like to associate with others, but can you do this?" p.165

  • Spoken

    "If you don’t like it, you do have the option to withdraw. Also, we’ll tell Sudou that he will be participating in the deliberation." p.165

  • Spoken

    "If Sudou himself consents, I will approve it. However, we cannot allow that many people. Only a maximum of two may sit in on the deliberation. Please think on that carefully." p.165

  • Trait

    She tests the witness by making her imagine the room: "as if she were testing the girl." p.164–165

  • Caveat

    The narrator's judgment of her method, kept as his: "As our teacher, Chabashira-sensei should have understood what kind of girl Sakura was." p.164

Ch.6 open: the rules she releases one at a timep.192–193
16 entries · 12 quoted
  • Scene

    The faculty room before the hearing; Hoshinomiya tries to tag along and Chabashira pushes her back inside. p.191–192

  • Spoken

    "What are you doing this time?" p.192

  • Spoken

    "Whenever you sneak out, that’s around the time I start to feel suspicious." p.192

  • Spoken

    "Of course you can’t. You know that outsiders cannot participate." p.192

  • Spoken

    "Well then, shall we be going?" p.192

  • Spoken

    "Of course not. This school does have rather complicated rules, but in cases such as these a settlement is reached between the homeroom teacher of the class in question, the concerned parties, and the student council." p.192

  • Caveat

    The venue is disclosed last, after Horikita has already agreed to attend; the narrator calls it her habit of revealing "an important detail right at the last minute." p.192

  • Spoken

    "If you want to stop, now’s the time, Horikita." p.192

  • Scene

    The fourth-floor student council room; she enters first and sets the proceedings in motion. p.193

  • Spoken

    "Sorry we’re late." p.193

  • Spoken

    "Have you already met?" p.193

  • Spoken

    "This is Sakagami-sensei, the homeroom teacher for Class C. Now then." p.193

  • Spoken

    "This is the student council president." p.193

  • Spoken

    "Well then, I would now like to discuss the violent incident that occurred last Tuesday with the members of the student council, the involved parties, and their homeroom teachers. You may begin the proceedings, student council secretary Tachibana." p.193

  • Spoken

    "So, this is all by chance?" p.193

  • Caveat

    The question is aimed at the president’s claim of attending by choice; he does not answer it. p.193

Ch.6 §6.1: the witness she dismissesp.199–200
4 entries · 2 quoted
  • Spoken

    "Why are you in such a hurry, Sakagami-sensei?" p.199

  • Caveat

    When Sakagami moves to strike the witness, she agrees with him instead of resisting: "You’d think that Chabashira-sensei would ally herself with Class D, but that didn’t seem to be the case." p.199–200

  • Spoken

    "You’re certainly right that this is a waste of time, so I suppose we have no choice. You can come down now, Sakura." p.200

  • Limit

    The order is what forces Sakura’s outburst; the chapter does not say whether Chabashira expected it, and the narrator records only that she "ordered Sakura to leave, almost as if she’d lost interest." p.200

Ch.6 §6.1: the compromise she will not refusep.205–209
6 entries · 3 quoted
  • Spoken

    "Compromise?" p.205

  • Spoken

    "We seem to have already reached a logical conclusion. There’s no reason to refuse Sakagami-sensei’s proposal," p.206

  • Trait

    She treats the settlement as arithmetic rather than advocacy: the proposal is "certainly a reasonable compromise," and she says so in front of her own student. p.206

  • Spoken

    "If this matter required some extra time before retrial, then the student council president would have offered a sufficient grace period. In other words, the amount of time granted should be enough for this case. Extensions are only offered under special circumstances," p.209

  • Rule

    The disciplinary settlement is heard by the two homeroom teachers, the concerned students and the student council; the retrial window is fixed by the council and cannot be extended on request. p.192, 209

  • Relationship

    She warns Horikita she can still withdraw, then closes the only procedural door Horikita asks to reopen. — Horikita Suzune p.192, 209

Ch.7 §7.5: the empty seat and the unexpected endingp.243–246
9 entries · 5 quoted
  • Scene

    She arrives with Sakagami for the retrial and finds Ayanokōji’s seat empty. p.243

  • Spoken

    "What happened to Ayanokouji, Horikita?" p.243

  • Spoken

    "Not participating?" p.243

  • Caveat

    She stares at the vacant seat "as if the proceedings would be meaningless without him." p.243

  • Spoken

    "You’re withdrawing your complaint?" p.244

  • Trait

    She chuckles with "a thin smile, as if she found something amusing." p.244

  • Spoken

    "Oh, excuse me. I was just surprised because I hadn’t foreseen this. I figured we would argue all day until one side collapsed, or until we could propose an acceptable compromise. However, unbelievably, they’ve said that they want to withdraw the complaint." p.244

  • Rule

    She confirms the practical result to both sides: no suspension for Sudō, no punishment for Class C, with this incident as the example to remember next time. p.246

  • Spoken

    "Of course. The same goes for the students in Class C, naturally. Youthful zeal is a good quality. However, the next time you think about causing problems, you should remember this incident as an example. Do not forget. Okay?" p.246

Ch.7 §7.5: what she says to Sudōp.246–247
8 entries · 7 quoted
  • Spoken

    "I’m glad, Sudou," p.246

  • Spoken

    "Personally, I think you should have been punished," p.246

  • Spoken

    "The reason this incident occurred at all is because of your behavior. Who told the truth and who lied is trivial. It’s important that you don’t let this kind of thing happen again. You do understand that, yes?" p.246

  • Spoken

    "However, admitting your own faults isn’t ‘cool.’ So even if you acknowledge that your personality was partly to blame, you act tough. You get stronger. That’s fine. However, if you act like that, you won’t be able to make real friends. Eventually, Horikita will abandon you. She’ll leave." p.246–247

  • Spoken

    "There’s strength in admitting your mistakes, Sudou." p.247

  • Trait

    The page records it as the first time she has tried to reach out to one of her students as a homeroom teacher. p.247

  • Spoken

    "You shouldn’t make promises so casually. You’ll cause trouble soon again." p.247

  • Spoken

    "What do you think, Horikita? Do you think that Sudou will become a model student?" p.247

Ch.7 §7.5: the private lesson about Ayanokōjip.248–251
18 entries · 16 quoted
  • Spoken

    "So. What methods did you use, Horikita?" p.248

  • Spoken

    "Don’t try to dodge the question. They wouldn’t withdraw their complaint without a reason, would they?" p.248

  • Spoken

    "So it’s a secret, hmm? Well, allow me to change the question. Who came up with the strategy that defeated Class C?" p.248

  • Spoken

    "Ayanokouji isn’t here, so I’m a little curious." p.248

  • Spoken

    "I see. So you recognize that, hmm?" p.249

  • Spoken

    "His potential, hmm?" p.249

  • Spoken

    "There are various things to consider. But if you want to reach Class A, I’ll give you one bit of advice." p.249

  • Spoken

    "The students in Class D all have some kind of defect, more or less. To borrow an expression used by others in this school, Class D is an assemblage of people that could be called ‘defective products.’ You already understand this quite well though, don’t you?" p.249

  • Spoken

    "Well, what do you think is Ayanokouji’s defect?" p.249

  • Spoken

    "Oh? And what is it?" p.249

  • Spoken

    "He dislikes trouble, hmm? Is that what you perceive when you look at Ayanokouji?" p.249–250

  • Spoken

    "Well, Horikita. Let’s try to learn as much as we possibly can about the boy called Ayanokouji, without delay, shall we? Otherwise, it’ll be too late. You already seem to have fallen into Ayanokouji’s trap." p.250

  • Spoken

    "Why do you think Ayanokouji purposefully got scores of 50 points across on the board on his entrance examination? Why do you think Ayanokouji is helping you? Why do you think that Ayanokouji doesn’t put himself forward as a superior student, despite having superior ability? Is Ayanokouji Kiyotaka really someone who ‘dislikes trouble’?" p.250

  • Spoken

    "In my personal opinion, Ayanokouji is the most defective student in Class D." p.250

  • Spoken

    "Higher functioning products are more difficult to handle. If you misunderstand how to handle him, the class might be completely destroyed in short order." p.250

  • Spoken

    "Get to know the person called Ayanokouji. What is he thinking? What focal point does he base his actions around? What is his fatal flaw? There’s definitely an answer there." p.250

  • Caveat

    She says nothing more, and the page gives no reason for the warning beyond Horikita’s own unanswered question. p.251

  • Trait

    Her usual manner is "unaware of and unconcerned with her class," which is why the session itself is the surprise. p.251

Hirata Yōsuke

Ch.2 §2.1: decoding Horikita in publicp.17–18
6 entries · 1 quoted
  • Scene

    "The class leader, Hirata Yousuke, answered for her." — after Horikita bait-and-silences the class on "get something." p.17

  • Spoken

    "I believe that Horikita-san is referring to the deductions we incurred throughout April and May. In other words, we didn’t see a reduction in points for talking in class or being late." p.18

  • Trait

    Reads peers' half-thoughts accurately and translates them for the room without seeking credit; AK: "The sharp-witted Hirata hadn’t missed a beat. Splendid." p.17–18

  • Backstory

    Named by AK as an expert-advice pillar ("If it were Horikita or Hirata here, they’d probably be able to give expert advice.") — but unusable on Sudou's case because "Sudou hates Hirata." p.36–37

  • Relationship

    Still the object of Sudou's hatred from the fake-expulsion episode; Hirata is not in the crisis's inner circle at all. — Sudō Ken p.37

  • Relationship

    AK holds him up as the standard he himself declines to be. — Ayanokōji Kiyotaka p.36

Ch.3: the one standing voice for Sudoup.40–43
9 entries · 4 quoted
  • Spoken

    “Umm. So why hasn’t this issue been resolved already?” — AK calls it "a rather reasonable question", the first thing anyone asks Chabashira after the announcement. p.40

  • Spoken

    “I want to believe him.” p.43

  • Spoken

    “If a student in another class were casting doubt on him, I could understand it,” p.43

  • Spoken

    “But I think it’s wrong to doubt a fellow classmate right away. Shouldn’t friends do everything within their power to help someone in need?” p.43

  • Scene

    Stands in support of Kushida's appeal for Sudou: "Hirata, the class hero, uttered those words as he stood in support of Kushida. His appearance was dashing, and he clearly wasn’t drunk on the same anti-Sudou Kool-Aid as everyone else." p.43

  • Trait

    His stance converts the room by example, not argument — AK: "Starting with those three, we launched our investigation to prove Sudou’s innocence", and "Hirata, Kushida, and Karuizawa had attracted the rest of our class’s admiration." p.43

  • Limit

    The conversion is fragile and AK says so plainly: "Though they probably mocked him in their hearts, they would help Sudou a little bit" — the class follows the lead, it does not change its opinion. p.43

  • Scene

    After school he organizes the canvassing as a team: AK names "the Hero & Gal Team" that he leads with Karuizawa, searching campus themselves. p.54

  • Relationship

    Publicly defends the classmate who refuses him privately; the Vol. 2 ch2 note stands — Sudou will not ask him, so the defence costs Hirata nothing with Sudou and gains Sudou nothing with him. — Sudō Ken p.43

Ch.4 §4.0: the speech that wins the room and the recall nobody else hasp.66–72
6 entries · 3 quoted
  • Scene

    His team's canvass produced records, not results: "I heard that Hirata and his group hadn’t obtained any valuable information. They’d recorded the names of the people to whom they’d spoken, and occasionally taken notes on their cell phones." p.66

  • Spoken

    "Then it would be a good idea for everyone to start saving up again," — and the reason he gives: "It’s only been three months since we started here." — AK: "Our classroom hero didn’t waver as he delivered his magnificent speech. The girls immediately blushed in response." p.66

  • Spoken

    "I understand how you feel. However, it can be dangerous to cling to our points and lose sight of reality. The most important thing is to treasure our friends as much as possible." p.67

  • Scene

    The cost of that line on himself, watched by the class's worst judge: "Ike, who considered Hirata a goody two shoes, eyed him suspiciously. 'Even if Sudou was at fault?' ... However, Hirata nodded without a moment’s hesitation. It was as though he believed self-sacrifice was unimportant. Ike immediately looked downward, as if pressed under the weight of Hirata’s noble intentions." p.67

  • Spoken

    "You know, if you think back on it now, it was obvious from the start." — the one student who had already connected the school's signals: "Remember what our P.E. instructor Higashiyama-sensei said when we went to the pool? He said the student who got the best time during our first class would get 5000 points. That was a stepping stone to prepare us for other opportunities. It seems plausible, right?" p.71

  • Feat

    His memory of a teacher's throwaway promise is the evidence that the club-points track exists, and Ike cannot supply any of it: "Ike scratched his head and slumped. 'I don’t remember,' he said." p.71

Ch.4 §4.2–4.3: he will greet Sakura but not ask her, and he physically stops Sudoup.83–84, p.92–93
11 entries · 8 quoted
  • Spoken

    "Good morning, Ayanokouji-kun." — "Hirata called to me, his expression as fresh and friendly as usual. The sweet yet subtle fragrance of flowers always emanated from him." p.83

  • Spoken

    Why he does not go to the witness himself: "Me? No. I’m going to greet her, that’s all. I’ve wanted to talk to her since she’s always by herself, but as a guy I don’t want to come off as forceful or anything. Also, if I asked Karuizawa-san to talk with her, that might cause a few problems." p.83

  • Spoken

    "I think that for the time being, we’ll just wait for more information from Kushida-san." — the hero's plan is to let someone else do the asking. p.83

  • Spoken

    His reason for briefing AK at all: "Not for any particular reason. I guess it’s because you have a connection to Horikita-san. She doesn’t talk to anyone except for you, so I thought you’d pass it along." — AK: "There was no real reason for him to tell me any of this. I wasn’t really part of the team." p.84

  • Scene

    He invites AK into his own social life, girlfriend included: "Oh, that reminds me. If you’re up for it, we should hang out sometime soon. What do you think?" and "Are you fine with my girlfriend coming, too?" — and reads AK's refusal when AK himself cannot: "I’m sorry. Do you not really want to?" p.84

  • Scene

    When Sudou kicks a desk apart and walks on Kōenji, he is the only one who moves: "He was the only boy able to move in this awful situation. My heart pounded in my chest." p.92

  • Spoken

    "All right, that’s enough. Calm down, you two," — and he puts the blame on both sides in front of the class: "Sudou-kun. You certainly are part of the problem here. But Kouenji-kun, you’re in the wrong, too." p.92

  • Spoken

    "Stop it already. I don’t want to see my friends fight…" is Kushida's; his is the follow-up that picks a side for the record: "It’s just like Kushida says. I don’t know about Kouenji-kun, but I’m your ally, Sudou-kun." — AK: "Hirata was way too cool. It would’ve been fitting if he changed his name to 'Hero.'" p.93

  • Spoken

    His last line to Sudou is the school's argument, not his own feeling: "I’m going to stop this. Sudou-kun, you should act more mature. If you cause another huge disturbance, the school’s evaluation of you will only worsen. Right?" p.93

  • Spoken

    Then, alone with Kōenji after Sudou slams out: "Kouenji-kun. I don’t intend to compel you to help. But you were wrong to blame him." — and AK's conclusion from watching the pair: "Watching their bizarre interaction unfold, I realized that our class lacked unity." p.93

  • Limit

    He never asks the witness anything, never challenges Horikita's write-off of Sudou, and stops a fight by citing the school's evaluation — leadership that avoids being the one who pressures anyone. p.83–93

Ch.4 §4.7, §4.11: he is the name people reach for when a conversation is going badlyp.120, p.149
5 entries
  • Feat

    Kushida, on the phone about the corridor fight: "I couldn’t even move. Hirata-kun was so amazing, though." — Ayanokōji's jealous gloss on the praise: "Hearing her praise Hirata like that made me a little jealous." p.120

  • Feat

    The couple the class copies: "It seemed like many students were striving every day to couple up like Hirata and Karuizawa." p.120

  • Feat

    He is invoked as a teacher of social technique by the one person who watches him most closely: "What should I do? Please, Hirata. Teach me." p.149

  • Relationship

    She credits him and herself, in the same breath, for Class D's cohesion: "Class D was able to come together, thanks to you and Hirata." — Kushida Kikyō p.120

  • Limit

    In the second half of Chapter 4 he appears only as other students' measure of themselves; his own scenes ended at p.93. p.120–149

  • Caveat

    He is named once, in Horikita’s summary of pending rewards for the hearing — Kushida’s and his actions "would be rewarded as well" — and appears nowhere else in the chapter. p.246

Horikita Manabu

Ch.6 §6.1: the president at the head of the tablep.193–196
6 entries · 3 quoted
  • Scene

    The student council room on the fourth floor; he sits in the back and does not look at his sister as she enters. p.193

  • Spoken

    "Because I am quite busy, there are certain agenda topics upon which I will defer. As a general rule, though, I would prefer to attend to these issues, as I’m entrusted to lead this student council." p.193

  • Caveat

    Chabashira’s probe about whether his presence is chance does not move him at all; the narrator notes his record — Class A on entry, student council secretary, president by December of his first year on an overwhelming vote. p.194

  • Spoken

    "It would seem there’s no objection, given the arguments we’ve heard thus far." p.196

  • Spoken

    "Regardless which party called the other over, the fact remains that it was a one-sided fight between Sudou and the other students. We can clearly see that from the injuries they sustained. We have no choice but to come up with a conclusion based on that." p.196

  • Trait

    He decides the case on visible injuries and closes the evidentiary question before Class D has finished speaking; the narrator describes his intervention as wanting "to draw a conclusion as soon as possible." p.196

Ch.6 §6.1: the retrial and the expulsion warningp.208–209

  • Caveat

    It is the first time he looks directly at his sister in the hearing. p.208

  • Spoken

    "That’s enough. Continuing this discussion would just be a waste of time." p.208

  • Spoken

    "What I’ve learned today is that each side has an exact opposite claim. In that case, one side is propagating an extremely malicious falsehood." p.208

  • Spoken

    "I’ll ask you, Class C. Have you lied to us today?" p.208

  • Spoken

    "Well, what about you, Class D?" p.209

  • Spoken

    "Then we will regroup here for a retrial tomorrow at 4:00 PM. If by then it hasn’t been clearly established which party has lied, or in the event no one admits that they were at fault, we will pass judgment based on the evidence we’ve collected thus far. Of course, in that case we may have to consider the possibility of expulsion from this school. That is all." p.209

  • Rule

    The retrial is set for 4:00 PM the next day, and if neither side is proven to have lied or admits fault, judgment falls back on the collected evidence with expulsion as a possible outcome. p.208–209

  • Caveat

    The escalation is deliberate: the charge shifts from a fight to which class lied to the school. p.209

Ch.4 §4.1: she names the witness, and refuses the creditp.75–81
16 entries · 9 quoted
  • Scene

    She appears at AK's door unannounced, the first time in the volume she seeks him out: "Just then, my doorbell rang and a visitor appeared... While I considered who this new visitor might be, she peered through the door." p.75

  • Spoken

    "Have you made any progress finding the witness?" — AK: "asked Horikita, looking as if she already knew the response." p.75

  • Spoken

    "I’m only telling you this because it’s you, but I may have found out something—" — she stops mid-sentence on seeing shoes at the door and turns to leave; Kushida's wave forces her in. p.76

  • Spoken

    "I didn’t particularly plan on helping. You don’t appear to have found the witness yet, right?" p.76

  • Spoken

    Her verdict on Kushida's plan, given without being asked to join it: "I wouldn’t say it’s a bad plan. Provided you have sufficient time, your labors may actually bear fruit." p.76

  • Spoken

    "I’ll give you just one piece of advice, to help your pathetic odds," — then the advice itself: "It’s hard to see what’s right in front of you. If someone actually witnessed Sudou’s incident, then that person is close by." p.77

  • Spoken

    "Sakura-san." — then, on being pressed: "She’s the witness. She saw the incident." p.77

  • Spoken

    The whole method, in one answer: "When Kushida-san said that she was looking for a witness in class, Sakura looked down. A lot of students were staring at Kushida-san. Sakura was the only one who didn’t appear interested. She wouldn’t have acted that way without some connection to the incident." p.77

  • Feat

    AK's measure of what she did: "I hadn’t noticed that at all. I was genuinely impressed by Horikita’s observational prowess. She’d noticed a classmate’s very minor gesture." — and his summary of the chapter's turn: "While we’d been paralyzed by uncertainty, Horikita had stepped up and taken control." p.77

  • Spoken

    "No, Sakura-san is undoubtedly the witness. Her actions confirmed it. Although she may not admit to it, she is definitely the one." p.78

  • Spoken

    She rejects the rescue narrative outright: "Don’t misunderstand. I just didn’t want to waste more time looking for the witness, and letting other classes see us in such a shameful light. That’s all." and "You are free to interpret things however you wish, but I’m telling you that you’re wrong." p.78

  • Limit

    She will move on reasons but never on appeal — the difference between AK's point argument, which lands, and Ike's or Sudou's gratitude, which she calls wrong. p.73, p.78

  • Feat

    Ike's playful shove on the shoulder ends with her tossing him to the ground and putting him in a headlock: "Don’t touch me. This is your one warning. The next time you try it, I’ll despise you all the way to graduation." AK's note on the technique: "those weren’t the defensive moves of a normal girl. Since I knew that her older brother practiced karate and aikido, she might have learned something, too?" p.78

  • Position

    She leaves the second the room turns to teasing her — "Goodbye." — before anyone can convert her help into belonging. p.81

  • Relationship

    Kushida is the one who physically keeps her in the room and then the one who asks for her advice; she answers Kushida's plan with a judgement, not participation. — Kushida Kikyō p.76–80

  • Relationship

    She refuses to let him believe she did it for him, twice, in the same breath in which she hands him the witness. — Sudō Ken p.78

Ch.4 §4.2–4.3: she writes Sudou off out loud, and prices the witnessp.88–94
11 entries · 7 quoted
  • Spoken

    "My, how unfortunate. Kushida failed to persuade her." — spoken as Kushida's approach to Sakura collapses in front of the class; AK agrees: "Horikita was correct. If Kushida couldn’t do it, then I didn’t think that anyone else in our class would be able to." p.88

  • Spoken

Ch.4 §4.4: she invites Ayanokōji out, and decides on Class Bp.95–107
