    The pedagogical claim she makes for not saving him: “That was different. Even if we managed to miraculously prove his innocence, do you think he’ll mature? Saving him might have the opposite effect.” — AK's tag: "Her defiant glare seemed to say, Do you understand my point?" p.111

  • Feat

    She turns his own reasoning back on him in real time: “Knowing Sudou’s flawed personality, you realized from the very beginning how difficult it would be to prove his innocence, didn’t you? That way, it’s easier to think he’d be better off accepting his punishment. Especially better for those who hate him.” — AK's record of being caught: "I felt like I’d been backed against a wall, unable to run. If I tried to forcefully deny, she would just dig deeper." p.111

  • Spoken

    Her verdict on the rest of Class D, delivered as mercilessly as AK notes: “Kushida-san and Ike-kun and the others haven’t noticed at all, it seems. They simply believe in Sudou-kun, and want to save him from the lie for his sake and our class’s sake. They don’t understand the urgency of the situation.” p.111

  • Spoken

    “A little bit? So she realized it by herself, then?” and “You told her, didn’t you?” — AK: "She cornered me with her words. It was like being interrogated. A little scary." p.111

  • Spoken

    The accusation that he engineered the midterm operations: “You came up with the idea of getting the old test questions, and purchasing exam points. I can’t say I’m surprised. You do seem quite cunning at times, but…I’m dissatisfied.” p.111–112

  • Spoken

    Her full description of him, the volume's closest attempt at a characterisation of Ayanokōji by another student: “Honestly, there’s so much about you that I don’t understand. You’re a mystery. You’re the most difficult person to predict in our class. You’re versatile, yet you often idle your time away. You never seem to stay still. It’s as though you cannot be categorized.” p.112

  • Spoken

    Then the sentence he cannot deflect: “I guess what I should say is that you’re just blending in and hiding your true talents. You’re hiding in plain sight. You disgust me more than anyone else.” p.112

  • Limit

    She does not actually know what he did, and stops at a feeling: "I see. I wondered if being called out like that was normal. It seemed I’d taken the bait and gotten caught in Horikita’s trap. A small failure on my part." — AK's own accounting of the conversation's cost. p.112

  • Spoken

    Her Kōenji assessment, offered as the counter-example to his mysteriousness: “He’s surprisingly easy to understand. He studies well, is athletic, and gets excellent grades. His personality is the problem. In the end, I can easily categorize and summarize his behavioral problems in only a few words: he’s self-centered.” p.112

  • Trait

    She is candid only in private and only with one person; the entire volume's worth of her actual analysis of Class D — Sudou, Kushida, Ayanokōji, Kōenji — is delivered in his room in one sitting, and none of it survives as anything but privately held when she re-enters a classroom. p.110–113

  • Relationship

    AK's own note on the setting, which should not be over-read: "I wondered why I felt a little nervous being alone with Horikita behind closed doors." — Ayanokōji Kiyotaka p.110

  • Position

    AK's projection of her, the chapter's last word on her: "At this rate, when she reached adulthood, she’d probably become a teacher like Chabashira-sensei." p.113

Ch.4 §4.6–4.8: named by everyone, present nowherep.118–130
9 entries · 4 quoted
  • Feat

    The narrator's roster of who is actually carrying Class D, in his own words: "Actually, the ones who had to work especially hard were Horikita, Hirata, and Kushida." p.118

  • Feat

    The first name in his private list of students wrongly sorted into Class D: "Horikita, Kouenji, and Yukimura were undoubtedly three people who deserved to be at the top based on their academics alone." p.119

  • Spoken

    Kushida, relaying her conclusion to the one person Horikita will not speak to directly: "Horikita-san seems convinced that Sakura saw everything." p.129

  • Spoken

    Kushida's summary of her, offered as the standing question of the class: "Speaking of special, don’t you think that Horikita-san is way more special than someone like me? She’s great at studying and excels at sports. It makes me wonder why she’s in Class D." p.130

  • Spoken

    Kushida's probe about her: "But doesn’t she behave normally with you, Ayanokouji-kun?" p.130

  • Spoken

    How Ayanokōji describes her to a third party, which is the only version of her Kushida receives: "When I think of my relationship with Horikita, it’s like there’s a wall between us. Or perhaps I should say that’s the extent of our relationship, if you understand what I’m getting at." p.130

  • Feat

    His withheld verdict on her exceptionalism, kept off the phone: "Horikita wasn’t special. She belonged to a unique class of people. I kept quiet, though, afraid that if I badmouthed her she’d find out about it." p.130

  • Limit

    The plan that would place her beside the witness is cancelled before it is proposed, by the person organising the errand: "While taking Horikita along would probably be the best option, it was delusional to think that Horikita and Kushida would spend their day off together." p.129

  • Caveat

    All of this is secondhand. The second half of Chapter 4 contains no Horikita scene; her last appearance is alone with Ayanokōji in his room, and the class's entire summer-facing strategy is discussed without her in the room where it is decided. p.118–130

Ch.6 §6.1: frozen, then roused, then cross-examiningp.193–200
16 entries · 12 quoted
  • Caveat

    Her brother’s presence undoes her before testimony begins: "Horikita —Horikita the younger sister, I mean—couldn’t hide her trembling," and the narrator concludes their kinship makes the room "extremely disadvantageous." p.193–194

  • Spoken

    "Hyah?!" p.197

  • Spoken

    "W-wait. S-stop, stop!" p.197

  • What happens

    She is physically roused by the narrator’s tickling and returns to the room glaring "with startling intensity." p.196–197

  • Spoken

    "Tch…" p.197

  • Spoken

    "Excuse me. May I ask a question?" p.197

  • Spoken

    "Earlier, you said that Sudou-kun called you over to the special building. But who exactly did Sudou call, and why?" p.197

  • Spoken

    "Please answer." p.197

  • Spoken

    "So then, why exactly were you in the special building, Ishizaki-kun? You’re not on the basketball team, so you have no connection to this case. I’d think your presence there would be rather odd." p.197

  • Spoken

    "So in other words, you felt the situation might turn violent?" p.198

  • Spoken

    "I see. So you brought along Ishizaki-kun as your bodyguard, since he was reputed to be rather good at fighting. Just in case there was an emergency." p.198

  • Spoken

    "I do have some knowledge of martial arts, if only to a certain extent. I understand that when you’re fighting against multiple enemies, victory becomes exponentially more difficult. So I don’t understand how you were defeated so handily, how the fight could be so one-sided, when you had a skilled fighter like Ishizaki-kun with you." p.198

  • Spoken

    "The primary factor in triggering a fight is the collision of the ‘energy’ between opponents. In the event that you don’t have any intention of fighting, or that you’re nonviolent, the probability that you’ll be hurt should be very low. Especially when there are three of you." p.198

  • Feat

    Her cross-examination turns the witness list into a contradiction: if Ishizaki came as a bodyguard because Sudō was reputed violent, a rehearsed nonviolent group should not have been beaten so one-sidedly. p.197–199

  • Caveat

    The narrator credits the method as "very objective, grounded in evidence, rules, and her own logic," and notes Komiya answers it with his actual injuries rather than with argument. p.198

  • Spoken

    "It’s true that Sudou injured the other students. However, Class C started the fight. There is one student witness who saw the entire incident and can attest to this." p.199

Ch.6 §6.1: the false concession, then the refusalp.207–209
11 entries · 8 quoted
  • Spoken

    "I understand…" p.207

  • Spoken

    "I think that Sudou, who caused the incident, has a problem. He doesn’t ever stop to consider his actions, which inconveniences everyone around him. He has a history of getting in fights. He’s the type to raise his voice and fists right away whenever something displeases him. In the event of an uproar such as this, well, it should be obvious who caused it." p.207

  • Spoken

    "You need to understand, Sudou. Your attitude caused all of this." p.207

  • Spoken

    "This is why I wasn’t motivated at all to help Sudou-kun in the beginning. I knew that even if I forced myself to lend a hand, he would just repeat the same mistakes again and again." p.207

  • Feat

    The closing statement is a trap: she lets Sakagami treat it as surrender, then refuses to sit, and the silence is what gives her the floor back. p.207–208

  • Spoken

    "He should reflect on his actions. However, not in this particular case. When I say he should reflect, I mean that he should look back on his past actions. In regards to this particular incident, however, I don’t think Sudou-kun did anything wrong. This was not an unfortunate event that occurred merely by chance. I’m convinced that this was a deliberate move made by Class C. I have absolutely no intention of meekly accepting defeat." p.208

  • Spoken

    "If you did not understand, I will say it once again. We claim that Sudou-kun is completely innocent. Therefore, we cannot accept his suspension from school, even for just one day." p.208

  • Spoken

    "Sudou-kun is the victim, just as the witness testified. Please do not make any errors in your judgment." p.208

  • Caveat

    The reversal is made for Sakura’s sake as much as Sudō’s: the narrator guesses she "had to be brave in front of Sakura." p.208

  • Spoken

    "Would be possible to have a little more time before we reconvene?" p.209

  • Caveat

    The line answering the president’s question to Class D — "I haven’t lied. Everything we’ve said has been the truth." — is untagged in the translation; it is recorded as Class D’s answer, not attributed to her by name on the page. p.209

Ichinose Honami

Ch.4 §4.4: she walks into the special building alone and offers Class B's helpp.102–107
23 entries · 15 quoted
  • Scene

    Her entrance is unannounced and physically striking to the narrator: "We turned in response to the unexpected voice. A beautiful girl with strawberry-blonde hair stood behind us. I recognized her, though I hadn’t ever spoken to her before. She was a student from Class B, Ichinose. Rumor had it she was a superb student." p.102

  • Spoken

    “Hey, you two. What are you doing?” p.102

  • Spoken

    “Sorry to interrupt you like that. Do you have a moment? Oh, if you happen to be in the middle of a so-so date, though, I’d like you to break it up right away.” p.102

  • Spoken

    “Ha ha, I see. This place is a little too hot to be a date spot.” p.103

  • Spoken

    She has been watching Class D without speaking to it: “I’ve met you twice before. We didn’t talk directly, though. I remember seeing you in the library once, too.” p.103

  • Spoken

    “I have a very good memory, you know.” — AK's receipt of it as a backhanded comment on his own unremarkableness. p.103

  • Spoken

    Her reason for being there is the case itself: “I thought that surely there’d be something here related to the brawl. I wasn’t here yesterday when Class B heard about the witness. Later, I heard that Class D was looking for evidence of Sudou’s innocence.” and “Hmm, a connection? Well, I’m not connected. When I heard about what happened, I had some doubts. So I thought I’d come here myself to take a look. Would you mind filling me in?” p.103–104

  • Spoken

    She takes refusal as data, not as an insult: “Guess not, huh? Well, if the other classes were interested…” p.104

  • Spoken

    To Horikita's accusation of an ulterior motive: “Ulterior motive? You think we’re working in the shadows to undermine both C and Class D?” — "Ichinose wore a baffled expression. 'Do you need to be that on guard? I’m really just curious, that’s all.'" p.104

  • Spoken

    Her first principle, aimed at Class D's own logic: “So that’s what happened. This story hasn’t made its way to Class B yet. Hey, isn’t this a pretty big problem, though? It doesn’t really matter who lied since it’s an issue about violence, right? Shouldn’t you be hurrying to uncover the truth?” p.105

  • Spoken

    The question she asks instead of taking a side: “So you believe Sudou-kun because he’s your classmate. And your friend, of course, that much is obvious. So Class D is in an uproar because Sudou is being falsely charged, right?” p.105

  • Rule

    The answer she gets back, from one of the two Class D students — the text does not say which: “I’d report on it honestly. After all, covering for a lie would only come back to haunt us later.” — and her reaction is agreement, not argument; AK's own line follows, "Yeah, okay. I think so, too." p.105

  • Spoken

    She does not take the dismissal personally: “Are you finished? You heard what you wanted.” is Horikita's; her answer is “Hmm. Yeah, how about I help you out? To look for the witness, or whatever. You’re bound to get further with more people, right?” p.105

  • Spoken

    Her argument for intervening in another class's discipline case, stated as system logic: “Are Classes D and B completely unrelated to each other? We don’t know when these cases will spring up, or whom they’ll involve. Since the classes are in constant competition, there’s always the chance that this will happen. This was the merely the first case. If the party that lied wins, that’d set a bad precedent. Also, I personally can’t turn away now that I know what happened.” (sic — "the merely") p.106

  • Feat

    She prices her own value honestly, including the downside: “If Class B works with you, wouldn’t that dramatically improve your credibility? Though I suppose the opposite could be true, too. Class D may suffer greater consequences if the truth’s uncovered…” and “What do you think? I don’t believe it’s a bad suggestion.” p.106

  • Position

    She is taken on: Horikita says yes, gives her own name first, and AK records the result — "We had unexpectedly made Ichinose of Class B’s acquaintance and accepted her as an ally. There was still the risk that this could lead to bad things. No matter what, things were going to change." p.106–107

  • Spoken

    She knows Ayanokōji's name the moment Horikita gives her own: “It’s nice to meet you, Horikita-san. And you too, Ayanokouji-kun.” — against AK's assumption a minute earlier that "she probably didn’t even know my name. I was only one of many students to her." p.107

  • Spoken

    Her reading of Class D's witness problem: “Well, that means there isn’t another witness. I mean, I guess someone from another class might have witnessed it, but it isn’t likely.” p.107

  • Rule

    She is the source of the class-points half of the club rule, which Class D's own teacher had never given them: “the school evaluates club and philanthropic activities, right? So if he enters a tournament and does well, Sudou-kun could earn points. Those’ll be tied to your class points, too. Wait… Did you guys not know that? Did your teacher not tell you?” p.107

  • Spoken

    Her diagnosis of the school's teaching staff: “Your homeroom teacher is kind of weird.” / “She doesn’t seem motivated to tell us anything. She’s completely apathetic. Some teachers are like that.” / “Did you know that the school evaluates homeroom teachers when their class graduates?” p.107

  • Trait

    She arrives with nothing to gain, says so twice, is called a liar's ally to her face, and still ends the afternoon as Class D's first outside partner — the volume's first Class B agent and the counter-example to Horikita's theory that nobody helps for free. p.102–107

  • Relationship

    Horikita suspects her on sight and hires her within the same conversation; the two are the pair who decide, over Sakura's silence, to bring another class in. — Horikita Suzune p.104–107

  • Caveat

    This is her first direct contact with Ayanokōji in the volume; every fact about her here comes from what she says in one afternoon, and no Class B student corroborates any of it. p.102–107

Ch.4 §4.4–4.6: what Class B's representative actually doesp.108–115
15 entries · 5 quoted
  • Spoken

    The reason her own teacher is different, given as a third party's motto: “Our homeroom teacher, Hoshinomiya-sensei, says it like it’s her motto. She says she wants to do her best because the homeroom teacher for Class A gets a special bonus. Looks like it’s pretty different for you guys.” p.108

  • Spoken

    “I think it would probably be good for us to meet up and discuss things.” — Horikita's answer, which she takes without offence: “I never thought I’d receive aid from the enemy.” p.108

  • Spoken

    “This seems like a problem we need to address before we can fight. We’re not really on equal footing, are we?” — the clearest statement in the volume of what the class-rank system does between classes. p.108

  • Spoken

    She says the quiet part about Class D's teacher out loud: “I’m envious of your relationship with your homeroom teacher. And your class environment.” p.108

  • Feat

    Horikita, who has just insulted her, is charmed: "Ha ha ha, that’s probably true. You’re pretty interesting." p.108

  • Scene

    Her contact details are exchanged on her initiative: “How about we exchange contact information, so things can progress smoothly?” — and AK learns her given name in the process: "Also, I learned that Ichinose’s first name was Honami—an unnecessary bit of information." p.109

  • Position

    Her reliability is measurable: "Every time she wanted permission to try something she’d contact us, even though she’d already said to leave things to her. I didn’t think she needed to limit herself so severely." p.110

  • Scene

    A month after the entrance exam she is already her class's channel to the school's staff: "Thank you very much. It’s been a pleasure." — she has been at the dormitory manager's desk handling other students' complaints: “Some people from my class wanted to make requests about their dorms. I gathered everyone’s thoughts on the matter and told the dorm manager. Stuff like water usage, noise, and so on.” p.114

  • Position

    Class B's self-organisation is the fact this scene exists to reveal: “Class Rep? Why Class Rep?” — “I’m my class’s representative. It seems like that, anyway.” and “No, that’s just something Class B set up on its own. I think it’s good to have some assigned roles, right?” p.114–115

  • Rule

    The full informal officer set, in her own words: “Whether or not they serve a function is a different question, but we do have other roles for formality’s sake. Stuff like vice class representative, and secretary. They could be more useful when we have a cultural fair, or sports festival, or something. We could try deciding things on the spot, but that might lead to trouble.” p.115

  • Trait

    AK's read of why she holds the role: “Most people wouldn’t want to be class representative. They’d be forced to do annoying stuff... However, with Ichinose taking the initiative for Class B, she probably didn’t foist things on others. I’d bet she handled her duties smoothly.” p.115

  • Spoken

    She refuses the grandeur of it: “Do you think it’s weird? It’s all purely informal. Besides, there are quite a few troublemakers. We have to deal with a lot of stuff.” — AK's note: "As she said, 'We have to deal with a lot of stuff,' Ichinose laughed." p.115

  • Scene

    Her physical entrance in this section is written by AK as attraction; it is his narration, not her characterisation: "She had beautiful long, wavy hair and big eyes. The second button of her blazer strained over her large chest. Her upright posture matched her dignified personality. I found myself more attracted to her cool temperament than how cute she was." p.114

  • Relationship

    She is the first person outside Class D Horikita will negotiate with, and the negotiation is conducted entirely by Horikita's hostility plus Horikita's yes. — Horikita Suzune p.108–110

  • Relationship

    Two weeks in, she greets him first, knows his name, and walks to school with him — while he is still cataloguing her as an asset with unclear motive. — Ayanokōji Kiyotaka p.114–115

But if students are ranked by overall ability, doesn’t that mean that the lower classes are at an overwhelming disadvantage?" p.119

  • Spoken

    "If we were competing as individuals, sure. But this is a competition between classes, right? If you simply put all of the superior students into Class A, then doesn’t that mean the rest of us have virtually no chance of succeeding?" p.119

  • Spoken

  • Spoken

  • Spoken

    "Has a girl ever confessed her feelings to you?" — the question she stops herself from explaining. p.121

  • Trait

    She works out the class-sorting problem in front of a stranger from the worst class in the school, offers her conclusion to him, and refuses to treat it as leverage: the only structural analysis of the four-class system a student gives all volume arrives unprompted and unguarded. p.119–120

  • Limit

    She asks the question she cannot follow up on: "Oh no, I’m sorry. It’s nothing." — and his note on it: "It didn’t look like it was nothing." p.121

  • Spoken

    "It’s rather early to draw a conclusion. We’ll give you one last chance. There’s only one way to save both Class C and Class D." p.232

  • Spoken

    "There’s only one way to resolve this issue. Tell the school that you wish to withdraw your complaint. If you do that, the school will not bring forward the security camera footage. If there’s no complaint, no one can be punished. Additionally, if the security footage is never brought up, Class D benefits, too. As we already know, if the security footage were brought produced, Sudou would still face some degree of punishment. In other words, Class C and Class D can come to a compromise together. The school can’t investigate if they can’t watch the video and see that you lied, right?" p.232

  • What happens

    She refuses to let Ishizaki call anyone and gives him no time to think. p.232

  • Spoken

    "Well, you’re not being very cooperative. Therefore, we have no choice but to prepare. We’ll have the school confirm the security footage right away, and you’ll be expelled." p.232

  • What happens

    She walks the three Class C students to the student council room, sandwiched between her and Ayanokōji. p.233

  • Spoken

    "Whew! I feel so much better! Thanks so much! Thanks for giving me such a huge role! I was so happy!" p.234

  • Spoken

    "Ha ha ha, yeah. I guess so. But the case is closed, eh?" p.234

  • What happens

    She and Kanzaki bought the cameras, and with the Professor installed them during the lunch break. p.234

  • Spoken

    "When the day comes, you guys will probably become formidable rivals for Class C, Ayanokouji-kun." p.234

  • Spoken

    "If Horikita-san were in Class B, we might have gotten to Class A right away." p.235

  • Spoken

    "I’ll definitely return the points I borrowed. Just tell me when you want them." p.235

  • Spoken

  • Spoken

    He recruits AK into the girlfriend contest and offers to set him up; AK's only counter is "Isn’t it kind of pathetic that you’re trying to introduce me to girls when you don’t have a girlfriend yourself?" and Ike can only answer "W-well…yeah." — Ayanokōji Kiyotaka p.52

  • Relationship

    Co-dependent rivals for the same girl — they trade fantasies, check each other for "outdone" moves, and leave AK as the audience. — Yamauchi Haruki p.50–52

Ch.4 §4.0 & §4.2: she wants Class A out loud, and is judged too blunt for Sakurap.66–67, p.83

Ch.4 §4.7: she survives in the volume as a standard, not a participantp.120

  • Feat

    "It seemed like many students were striving every day to couple up like Hirata and Karuizawa." — the only mention of her in the second half of Chapter 4, and it is a comparator other students are measured against. p.120

  • Caveat

    After her p.83 exclusion from the witness interview she has no scene, no line and no decision in Chapter 4. p.120

Kōenji Rokusuke

Ch.2 §2.2 (lunch): the balance that bought him out of the cafeteriap.20
6 entries
  • Scene

    Lunch in the Class D room: Horikita, Ayanokōji and Kōenji are the students still at their desks while the rest have gone to eat — "a few students were still all alone". p.20

  • Backstory

    AK's account of how his social calendar changed: "On first starting here, he’d enjoyed the company of girls from other classes and grade levels. However, once he ran low on points, he began to spend most of his time in the classroom." p.20

  • Scene

    "He was currently completely lost in examining his face in a hand mirror, his daily routine." p.20

  • Trait

    AK's correction of the usual reading of him: "He didn’t love solitude, but rather, loved himself and cared little for others. I respected that he didn’t seem bothered at all by being alone." p.20

  • Backstory

    "He was the sole heir to the Kouenji conglomerate group, one of the largest corporations in Japan." — AK repeats the fact here as background for why Kōenji is unbothered; his own dismissal of class rank belongs to Volume 1 and is recorded there. p.20

  • Caveat

    What he eats, and whether he buys anything at all in July, is never stated; the text here says only that he has stopped leaving the classroom to be entertained. p.20

Ch.4 §4.3: he speaks to Sudou's face without looking at himp.92–93
8 entries · 4 quoted
  • Scene

    His intervention is announced by the mirror, not the man: "The voice came from a boy checking his reflection in a hand mirror to fix his hair. It was Kouenji Rokusuke, an especially odd guy even amongst the remarkably strange people of our class." p.92

  • Spoken

    “Don’t you think it would’ve been better if you’d been expelled? Your existence is far from beautiful. No, in fact, I could say your life is an ugly one, Red Hair-kun.” — he nicknames Sudou by his hair, the same detail the class's witness used for him. p.92

  • Scene

    AK on the delivery: "Kouenji didn’t even look at Sudou as he spoke. It was as if this were a soliloquy." p.92

  • Spoken

    “It would be inefficient for me to tell you, no matter how many times I did. If I know that you’re dim-witted, then it doesn’t matter how many times I try to lecture you, does it?” p.92

  • Spoken

    “Puh. I don’t think I’ve experienced being wrong since I was born. You must be mistaken.” — to Hirata, the one classmate who tells him he is in the wrong. p.93

  • Spoken

    “Unfortunately, I’ve never experienced being wrong. Not once in my entire life. Oh, looks like it’s about time for my date. Well, if you’ll excuse me.” — he leaves mid-argument on a scheduled date; no date is ever shown. p.93

  • Feat

    What his two sentences cost: a desk is kicked apart, Sudou walks on him, and the class's last organised effort to save anyone collapses into Hirata physically holding Sudou back. p.92

  • Trait

    He is the only student in the volume who treats expulsion as a preference rather than a threat, and he says it to the one student whose expulsion risk he cannot lose anything from. p.92–93

Ch.4 §4.6, §4.8: he exists in the rest of the chapter as other people's summaryp.119, p.129
4 entries · 1 quoted
  • Feat

    In the narrator's private list of students who were wrongly sorted into Class D: "Horikita, Kouenji, and Yukimura were undoubtedly three people who deserved to be at the top based on their academics alone." p.119

  • Spoken

    Kushida, reopening the corridor incident on the phone: "That reminds me. It was really scary when Kouenji-kun and Sudoukun looked like they were about to start fighting." — printed as one word; kept as the translation has it. p.129

  • Limit

    In Ayanokōji's estimate of what that fight would have become: "Kouenji always seemed laid-back, but if Sudou started taking swings at him, he would fight. If that happened, it’d be a disaster." p.129

  • Position

    He has no scene of his own after p.93; the second half of Chapter 4 keeps him as a recollection and a hypothesis. p.119–129

Ch.2 §2.4: Sudou's crisis conference — angel mode with an icy seamp.27–38
37 entries · 31 quoted
  • Scene

    Knocks during AK's kettle moment; "Kushida, the Madonna of Class D, poked her head through the entrance. She looked as cute as ever." p.28

    "S-suspended?" p.29

  • Spoken

    "Ha ha, I’m only joking! Sudou-kun wouldn’t go that far." (the joke lands wrong — Sudou's shock proves the secret is real) p.30

    "I guess that their plan is to severely punish Sudou for punching them, huh?" p.33

  • Spoken

    "Let’s just come out and ask those three Class C students to be honest. If they feel like what they did was wrong, then surely they’d feel guilty about it. Right?" p.34

  • Spoken

    "There are two ways we can prove your innocence, Sudou-kun. The first way is to simply get those boys in Class C to admit that they lied. Since you weren’t at fault, it’s probably best to get them to acknowledge that." p.34

  • Spoken

    "The other idea is to find the eyewitness you talked about, Sudou-kun. If someone happened to see the fight, then we should be able to get to the heart of the matter." p.35

  • Spoken

    "Asking people one by one? Or we could simply address each class as a whole," p.35

  • Spoken

    "H-huh? You don’t want us to tell anyone?" p.35

  • Spoken

    "But won’t the students in Class C talk about how Sudou-kun was violent? I mean, that would work in their favor, I’d think." p.35

  • Spoken

    "Sudou-kun, I think it would be better if you kept your distance," (Sudou must stay away while accused) p.35

  • Spoken

    "I don’t think it’s being dumped on us. We want you to rely on us, Sudou-kun. I don’t know how much we can do, but we’ll try our best. Okay?" p.36

  • Spoken

    "I have a few things I wanted to talk to you about, Ayanokouji-kun. You didn’t seem very enthusiastic about helping Sudou-kun." p.36

  • Spoken

    "Maybe, but Sudou-kun came to you, Ayanokouji-kun. He came to you before Horikita-san, Hirata-kun, or even Ike-kun." p.36

  • Feat

    The mask slips mid-conversation: "For an instant, Kushida’s glare turned icy, which perplexed me." — then she recomposes into advice. p.37

  • Spoken

    "I think it might be better if you made more effort to blend in, Ayanokouji-kun," p.37

  • Spoken

    "Of course. We’re friends. So what will you do, Ayanokouji-kun?" p.37

  • Spoken

    "Do you think Horikita-san will help us?" p.37

  • Spoken

    "I know that you’re trying to dodge the question, but you’ll help, too. Won’t you, Ayanokouji-kun?" p.37

  • Spoken

    "You won’t be useless. I’m sure you’ll be useful, somehow." p.37

  • Spoken

    "So what should we do? Sudou-kun said that it wouldn’t help, but I think it’d be good to talk with the three students he fought. To tell you the truth, I’m friends with Komiya-kun and the others. Therefore, it might be possible to persuade them. Hmm, it may be dangerous, though." p.37

  • Spoken

    "Well then, I guess looking for the eyewitness is our best bet." p.38

  • Trait

    Structured empathy: jokes to defuse, then imposes an agenda (two proof routes), volunteers her cross-class network (Komiya et al. in C) as a tool, and pins AK to a commitment he keeps dodging. p.29–38

  • Limit

    Her pressure only works socially — AK admits "Like right now, I didn’t have the guts to promise that I’d help."; her icy glare is the only lever that lands. p.37

  • Relationship

    Officially takes his side, secures his cooperation with warmth, keeps the secrecy bargain. — Sudō Ken p.35–36

  • Relationship

    Escalates one-on-one after Sudou leaves; demands he publicly invest in the case; he internally recalls her "I hate you" warning while complying outwardly. — Ayanokōji Kiyotaka p.36–38

Ch.3 open: she takes the class's anger apart in front of everyonep.41–43
9 entries · 3 quoted
  • Scene

    With the uproar starting to get out of hand, AK's note on her motive: "Naturally, Kushida didn’t want this to happen." — she is defending the class's cohesion, not Sudou. p.41

  • Spoken

    “Everyone, can you please listen to what I have to say?” p.42

  • Spoken

    “What our teacher said seems to be true. Sudou-kun may have been in a fight. However, Sudou-kun was dragged into it.” p.42

  • Scene

    She tells the room Sudou's whole account — the regular-player nomination, the envious clubmates, the threat, the self-defence: "Kushida conveyed yesterday’s story to the whole class." p.42

  • Spoken

    “I’d like to ask you all again. If anyone knows someone who saw what happened, whether it’s someone in class, a friend, or an upperclassman, please tell me. You can contact me at any time. I would sincerely appreciate it.” p.42

  • Feat

    AK measures the difference between her and the teacher saying the same thing: "Even though she’d basically said the same thing as Chabashira-sensei, the class had a very different response. She had an innate ability to connect with people. Her presence glowed so bright that I could almost feel it." p.42–43

  • Limit

    Charisma alone does not carry it: after Yamauchi's accusation, "Kushida’s appeal for Sudou’s innocence didn’t seem to have reached anyone." It takes Hirata standing up — and then Karuizawa — to turn the room. p.43

  • Relationship

    He backs her appeal publicly; AK credits the pair of them plus Karuizawa with converting the class, which is the closest the volume comes to the two leaders acting together. — Hirata Yōsuke p.43

  • Relationship

    AK's paired mechanism, stated in the same breath: "If Kushida lived by the softness of her heart, then Karuizawa lived by the strength of her will." — Karuizawa Kei p.43

Ch.3 §3.1: keeping Ayanokōji inside the groupp.44, p.47
2 entries
  • Scene

    She assembles the lunch table — AK, Horikita, Ike, Yamauchi, Sudou — by asking him directly: "When lunchtime came, Kushida had invited me with a smile. She’d said, 'Want to get lunch?' I’d said okay, of course. I mean, I didn’t have any choice in the matter." p.44

  • Scene

    She leaves the table early to work her own network: “Ah, sorry. I have to go for a bit. I’m going to try asking my upperclassmen friends if they saw anything.” p.47

Ch.3 §3.2–3.3: Horikita, the lesson, and Class Bp.54–63
21 entries · 14 quoted
  • Spoken

    “Are you really heading back? Horikita-san?” p.54

    “You will…help us out, won’t you?” (stopping AK from slipping out; AK: "I couldn’t refuse. Slightly upturned eyes + pleading = lethal.") p.54

  • Spoken

    “I want to get Horikita-san to help us out this time. Can you try asking her again?” p.55

  • Spoken

    “Yeah. I want to chase after her. If Horikita-san helps, I think she’ll make a big difference.” p.55

  • Scene

    She takes AK's arm in front of Ike and Yamauchi and walks him out with her; Ike and Yamauchi are left behind because, as AK notes, "You certainly couldn’t claim those two were friendly with Horikita." p.55

  • Spoken

    “I really want you to help us with Sudou’s case. Is that possible?” p.56

  • Spoken

    “I know you did, but…but, I think this is necessary to reach Class A.” p.56

  • Spoken

    “W-wait. What do you mean? Wasn’t Sudou-kun dragged into the fight?” p.57

  • Spoken

    “Sudou-kun is a perpetrator? Is that…true?” p.57

    “I don’t agree with abandoning Sudou-kun just to punish him. If she’s dissatisfied with him, I think she should at least talk to him directly. That’s what friends would do.” p.60

  • Spoken

    “Okay. I understand. I’ll follow your advice, Ayanokouji-kun.” p.60

  • Trait

    AK's definition of her, offered as a limit on himself: "She would extend her hand to a friend in need as many times as it took. It sounds simple, but it was actually difficult. Only someone like Kushida could do such a thing." p.60

  • Feat

    She is moved mid-conversation and lets it show: "Kushida arched her back and stretched; I’d changed her thinking." — then converts the new position into action ("Okay, let’s go hunt for the witness.") p.60

  • Spoken

    “No, I’m sorry. I failed,” p.61

  • Spoken

    “Well then, let’s go to Class B right away!” p.61

  • Spoken

    “I also think you’re being too negative. I do think it’s a good idea to ask Class B, but I also think we should ask several people at once. If not, we may not find the witness in time.” p.62

  • Spoken

    “I’m sorry, Ayanokouji-kun.” (overruling his one-at-a-time plan with Ike and Yamauchi behind her, and apologising for it; AK withdraws: "Kushida’s plan had convinced me, so I withdrew my own.") p.62

  • Scene

    In the Class B room she is the only one of the four who moves: "Kushida was the only one who remained unfazed. In fact, she found her Class B friends and, with a smile on her face, waved to them and headed over." p.63

  • Feat

    AK's note on the transferability of her manner: "She chatted with people regardless of gender, exactly how she acted in Class D. No one was more jealous of this than Ike and Yamauchi." p.63

  • Scene

    She pitches Sudou's case to the roughly ten Class B students still in the room — the first cross-class advocacy of the volume. p.63

  • Limit

    Her network has a ceiling she cannot see: AK's whole plan for Class B rests on the class's self-interest, and she is running on friendship, which is why the report she brings back surprises him. (What the report is, the text withholds until the next chapter.) p.63

Ch.4 §4.0–4.1: she runs the search, and is the only one who can name Sakura's seatp.66–82
10 entries · 6 quoted
  • Spoken

    "As long as we can find a witness, it’s not impossible. Let’s keep trying our best, Ike-kun." — the pep line she gives the boy who has just declared the case hopeless. p.66

  • Feat

    Her method of remembering is exact: "It’s certainly true that Sudou-kun didn’t say he saw anyone. He said that he had a feeling that someone was there." — she corrects the class's summary of a claim three days old. p.74

  • Spoken

    She re-engineers the search from the witness to the witness's surroundings: "We might want to change our methods a little. For example, let’s look for someone who might have found a witness." — AK's judgement: "That wasn’t a bad idea." p.75

  • Scene

    Horikita arrives at the room and Kushida is the reason she stays: "Kushida peered over at the entrance, probably concerned that Horikita wouldn’t return. 'Ah, Horikita-san!' Kushida said. Kushida smiled and waved to Horikita." p.76

  • Spoken

    "If you didn’t come here to help, then why did you come?" — and then, instead of pressing: "Well, I’m happy if you just want to listen to us talk. I hoped you could give some advice." p.76

  • Scene

    She is the class's map of itself: "No, you’re both wrong. She sits diagonally ahead of Sudou-kun to the right." — AK: "Kushida corrected them, a slightly pouty look on her face." p.79

  • Spoken

    She defends a classmate she has never spoken to, in public, against Ike: "It’s not good to remember people based on something like that, Ikekun! That’s pathetic." p.80

  • Spoken

    The phone attempt fails and she names why it is a delicate thing: "No luck. The call didn’t go through. I can try again later, but this may be a delicate matter." and "She gave me her contact information, but she doesn’t know me very well. She might be confused if I tried to call her." p.80

  • Spoken

    She reads the room's failure before it happens and shrinks the plan to one person: "Well, I’ll try to talk to her myself tomorrow. I think she might be on her guard if a lot of people approach her." p.82

  • Relationship

    She holds Sakura's number and Sakura's seating position and none of Sakura's trust, and is the one student whose name she will not let Ike reduce to a body. — Sakura Airi p.80–82

Ch.4 §4.2–4.3: the approach fails in front of the whole classp.86–93
8 entries · 3 quoted
  • Scene

    "Class was over. Kushida stood up right as homeroom ended and walked over towards Sakura, who was quietly preparing to leave. Kushida seemed oddly nervous." p.86

  • Spoken

    "Sakura-san?" then "I wanted to ask you something, Sakura-san. Do you have a moment? It’s about Sudou’s case." p.86

    "Can you please make some time? This is important, so I’d like to talk to you. When Sudou-kun was involved in the incident, were you perhaps somewhere nearby?" p.88

  • Spoken

    "Can’t you please spare just a few minutes now?" — the third ask, the one that turns the conversation into a scene the room is watching. p.88

  • Limit

    AK's post-mortem of her judgement, in her own terms: "Kushida probably had expected this conversation to go more smoothly, and had gone into this situation thinking she wasn’t going to be rejected. That explained why this was currently falling apart." p.88

    She takes the blame for a broken camera that was not hers: "I-I’m sorry. It’s because I came up and talked to you so suddenly, that…" — and is answered flatly: "No, that’s not it. I was just careless, that’s all… Anyway, goodbye." p.90

  • Feat

    Even after failing she keeps the case alive by asking the quietest boy in the room: "Ayanokouji-kun, you’re Sudou-kun’s ally, aren’t you?" — "Well, it’s just that things seem to a bit tense. Everyone’s desire to save Sudou is fading." p.91

  • Relationship

    She uses his proximity as a resource twice in one day: the ally question, and the earlier instruction that he keep Horikita reachable (Hirata briefs AK about her because "she doesn’t talk to anyone except for you"). — Ayanokōji Kiyotaka p.91

Ch.4 §4.8: she phones him at night, and cannot finish what she startsp.128–131
17 entries · 11 quoted

  • Spoken

  • Spoken

  • Spoken

    What Horikita has concluded, carried by the one person Horikita will not speak to: "Horikita-san seems convinced that Sakura saw everything.   • Spoken

    "Class D was able to come together, thanks to you and Hirata. The fact that the boys and girls are separated plays a big part, too." p.130

    "But doesn’t she behave normally with you, Ayanokouji-kun?" p.130

    "Huh? Ah, yeah, it is. Why? What about it?" — he has asked whether her room is on the ninth floor. p.131

  • Feat

    Her gift with the class's outliers, described from the outside: "Kushida had done a wonderful job taking the proper first steps to earn Sakura’s trust." p.130

  • Limit

    The only silence the volume records her in: "Suddenly, Kushida fell silent. It was unexpected silence, without warning. Our conversation, which had been smooth up until now, came to a screeching halt." p.131

  • Limit

    She starts a confession three times and stops: "Hey—" then "I-If… Well… I… I—" then "No, it’s nothing." — and the recorder's verdict: "That sure didn’t seem like nothing…" p.131

  • Spoken

    "Well, I’ll see you the day after tomorrow, Ayanokouji-kun." — the call's last line, with the unfinished one still in the air. p.131

  • Trait

    She is the only classmate who tests his account of Horikita instead of accepting it — and his reaction to being read is discomfort rather than deflection: "I didn’t like being misunderstood by Kushida." p.130–131

  • Caveat

    She hesitates immediately after being asked for her floor number, and the volume never says whether the two are connected. What is recorded is only the sequence: the question, the halt, the swallowed sentence, the goodbye. p.131

  • Spoken

  • Spoken

    Her offer of a normal relationship, phrased as a complaint about speech: "you can talk to us normally, you know? If it’s okay with you. We’re in the same grade. It sounds a little weird when you speak so formally to us." p.145

  • Spoken

    "It’s okay, though. You don’t need to force yourself." p.145

  • Spoken

    The ask that finally gets the witness: "So, does that mean that you saw Sudou-kun fight with those other students?" and then "No, we do. Why did you decide to tell us now, though? I mean, I’m happy you did, but I don’t want you to force yourself. You don’t have to do this just because you’re grateful, you know?" p.146

  • Spoken

    "Is that really true? You’re not forcing yourself?" Kushida asked. — the same question twice in one minute, because the first answer was a shake of the head. p.146

  • Spoken

    "Thank you, Sakura-san. I’m sure Sudou-kun will be really happy." — "Kushida took Sakura’s hand." p.146

  • Feat

    The case the whole class failed to move in three weeks is closed by a hand-hold and two refusals to press: "I wondered if a new friendship had been born right here, right now. At any rate, we had Sudou’s witness." p.146

  • Trait

    She is the only person in Class D who gets things done by being with people rather than by being right about them; the volume records her succeeding at exactly the task Horikita wrote off as worthless. p.132–146

  • Relationship

    From a camera knocked over in a classroom (p.90) to a promise to testify in a shopping mall: the whole distance is crossed by Kushida refusing to make anything harder than it already is. — Sakura Airi p.138–146

  • Relationship

    She recruits him by asking, tests whether he has been left out of his own friend group, and lets him fill in a form; he is her one witness that she is capable of a plain conversation. — Ayanokōji Kiyotaka p.128–145

  • Limit

    Her instinct to close on Sakura stops short of the truth: she asks about the glasses, gets refused, and "Kushida seemed to feel that it would make trouble if she pressed the issue, so she kept quiet. Or rather, she didn’t ask anything else." p.144

Sakura Airi

Ch.1 "Sakura Airi's Soliloquy": the volume opens in her POVp.10
9 entries · 5 quoted
  • Backstory

    Whole first-person monologue chapter — the only non-Ayanokōji POV chapter opening in Vol. 2; foreshadows her role as "An Unexpected Witness" (Ch.4). p.10

  • Inner voice

    "I don't know how to interact with other people. I'm bad at talking while making eye contact. I'm horrible with crowds. I don't remember how or when I became so hopeless." p.10

  • Inner voice

    "However, one thing I do know for certain is that a person cannot live entirely in isolation. No matter how much I may love my solitude, I can't remain totally by myself. So, I came up with a solution. I would adopt a false face and hide my true self. Then, I wouldn't be completely honest, but I could be a version of myself. I could continue to live in this dark, lonely world." p.10

  • Trait

    Self-aware about her condition; frames the false face as a deliberate "solution," not an accident — same masking logic she later applies to photography. p.10

  • Inner voice

    "Is everyone else putting on a façade, just like me? Or do most people show their true selves to the outside world? Since I can't connect with people, I suppose there's no way for me to find out the answer. Therefore, I remain alone." p.10

  • Inner voice

    "I'm all right by myself. I'm all right with being alone." p.10

  • Inner voice

    "I… I want to connect with someone from the bottom of my heart." p.10

  • Voice

    Her interior voice: short declarative confessions, self-questioning, closing cadence "And so I will continue to live quietly, with my eyes downcast. Alone." p.10

  • Trait

    Paradox locked in at volume start: wants a heart-to-heart connection while believing connection is impossible for her — the engine of her Vol. 2 arc. p.10

Ch.2 opening: she stumbles on Sudou's three-on-one brawl (the witness birthed)p.12–13
11 entries · 5 quoted
  • Scene

    Evening of June 30, inside a school building with a staircase (the "special building," named Sudou's account p.30–31). While hunting for a good selfie spot, Sakura finds three male students writhing on the floor and a red-haired boy standing over them. p.12

  • Inner voice

    "The timing couldn’t have been worse. While searching for a place to take a good selfie, I stumbled into something. Even a certain famous, diminutive detective would have held his breath when witnessing such a tense situation." p.12

  • Inner voice

    "His right fist was covered in blood from the students he’d pummeled. This was the first brawl I’d ever witnessed." p.12

  • Feat

    Terror overrides etiquette but not habit: "Though I was terrified, I began capturing the scene with my camera. The shutter didn’t make a sound." p.12

  • Inner voice

    Panic freeze: "After taking the pictures, I asked myself what I was doing. I couldn’t think clearly in my panicked state... My legs didn’t obey my command to move, like I was paralyzed." p.12

  • Inner voice

    "I’d heard that that when someone was involved in a crisis, his or her body would lock up, exactly like what was happening now." (sic — doubled "that" in corpus) p.13

  • Inner voice

    "I’d hate it if someone were to spot me and involve me in this mess. If that happened, it would cast a dark cloud over my otherwise peaceful school life." p.13

  • Scene

    Sudou, sensing movement, calls "Is someone there?" toward her hiding spot; she escapes by seconds: "I’d successfully escaped by a hair’s breadth. If I’d lagged a mere two seconds, he probably would’ve seen me." p.13

  • Trait

    Document-first, confront-never instinct: she photographs the fight but flees rather than intervene or report; self-protection through invisibility. p.12–13

  • Relationship

    Unseen witness to his brawl; nearly discovered by him — she does not know his name-to-face link yet beyond "Sudou-kun" heard in his taunts. — Sudō Ken p.12–13

  • Scene

    §2.2 lunch, observed by Ayanokōji: still eating alone, hunched over a homemade bento; the glasses-and-plain-girl reputation ("At one time Ike had made a ruckus about how large her breasts were, but because she was considered plain, everyone quickly lost interest."). p.20

Ch.3: the witness sits in the room and says nothingp.40–65
7 entries
  • Scene

    Chabashira puts the ask to the classroom: "If anyone here witnessed the fight, please raise your hand." — "No students raised their hands in response to her question." p.41

  • Caveat

    This is inference, stated as inference: Sakura was in that homeroom (pp.40–43 is Class D's classroom, and she is a Class D student), but Chapter 3 never singles her out, never names her, and never says whether she looked up. All this page can assert is that no hand went up. p.41

  • Scene

    Kushida re-asks the same question in a form a witness could answer privately — "If anyone knows someone who saw what happened, whether it’s someone in class, a friend, or an upperclassman, please tell me. You can contact me at any time." — the one line in the chapter that would have been safe for her to answer, and she does not. p.42

  • Position

    She holds the only neutral visual record of pp.12–13 — silent-camera photographs she has not shown anyone — and Chapter 3 closes with it still hidden. p.40–65

  • Limit

    The window that her silence consumes is fixed and public: "We’ll make our final judgment next Tuesday, taking into account any eyewitnesses and evidence," and "In order to check for witnesses, every teacher will inform their classes of the details of this incident." The school is now actively canvassing every class in the year for her. p.41

  • Caveat

    AK, in a classroom with "about one third of our class remained in the room": "Suddenly, I felt a sensation, like someone was watching me. I turned around." The text never says who watched him and never resolves it inside the chapter. Do not read it as Sakura — it is an unanswered beat, not a link. p.62

  • Trait

    Her Chapter 1 creed ("And so I will continue to live quietly, with my eyes downcast. Alone.") is what the whole chapter quietly costs Sudou: invisibility works, and it works on the class's behalf too. p.40–65

Ch.4 §4.1: she is named, and stays a rumour to the people who sit near herp.77–82
16 entries · 5 quoted
  • Spoken

    "Sakura-san." — the first time anyone in Class D says her name out loud in the volume, spoken by Horikita in Ayanokōji's room. p.77

  • Spoken

    Horikita's proof, which is entirely about Sakura's own behaviour: "When Kushida-san said that she was looking for a witness in class, Sakura looked down. A lot of students were staring at Kushida-san. Sakura was the only one who didn’t appear interested." p.77

  • Position

    She is now identified as the witness by the one student who was watching the wrong student, and Horikita's confidence is absolute: "No, Sakura-san is undoubtedly the witness. Her actions confirmed it. Although she may not admit to it, she is definitely the one." p.77–78

  • Scene

    Nobody in the room can place her body in the room: Sudou and Yamauchi "exchanged looks. They didn’t seem to know who Sakura even was"; the seating is argued wrong twice before Kushida fixes it — "She sits diagonally ahead of Sudou-kun to the right." p.79

  • Spoken

    Sudou's admission of the class's real opinion of her: "Diagonally ahead to the right? I can’t remember at all. I’m sure there’s someone there, though." p.79

  • Trait

    AK's verdict on why that matters for the case: "Sakura certainly didn’t stand out very much. The fact we didn’t really know her was a problem." p.79

  • Spoken

    Ike, asked to describe her, offers one fact: "Would it help if I said she has the biggest boobs in class? Her boobs are insanely huge." — and Yamauchi converts it into a stereotype on the spot: "Oh, she’s that plain girl with glasses, right?" p.79–80

  • Scene

    Kushida defends her, in the only defence she gets in the chapter: "It’s not good to remember people based on something like that, Ikekun! That’s pathetic." p.80

  • Position

    Her phone goes unanswered when Kushida calls: "She listened to her phone ring for about 20 seconds, but then she shook her head and hung up." — AK's reading: "Sakura might just be pretending to be out, too." p.80

  • Spoken

    Kushida's account of what Sakura actually gave her: "She gave me her contact information, but she doesn’t know me very well. She might be confused if I tried to call her." p.81

  • Feat

    Her invisibility is total enough to make the room's memory unreliable: Sudou misnames her on the spot — "this Sakura, or Kokura, or whoever" — and Yamauchi disowns having ever confessed to her ("It wasn’t Sakura; it was a girl from the next class over. Not a gloomy and ugly girl like Sakura."), then drops the subject mid-sentence by claiming a message. p.78, p.82

  • Trait

    Even the class's most charitable description is an apology for her: "It’s certainly true that Sakura was plain, but she wasn’t ugly. I’d never gotten a good look at her face before, but she seemed to have very nice features. Even so, I couldn’t say that with complete confidence, probably because Sakura had so little presence." p.82

  • Scene

    The plan the chapter ends on is to let one girl approach her alone: "Well, I’ll try to talk to her myself tomorrow. I think she might be on her guard if a lot of people approach her," Kushida said — and AK's gloss: "If Kushida couldn’t manage it, then I doubted anyone else would be able to get through to Sakura." p.82

  • Relationship

    The only Class D student who ever looked at her closely is the one who does not like being looked at; Horikita's read of Sakura's downcast eyes is the chapter's hinge and Sakura never learns it happened. — Horikita Suzune p.77

  • Relationship

    Kushida holds her contact number, her seating position and the class's only defence of her, and still cannot reach her by phone. — Kushida Kikyō p.80–82

  • Caveat

    All of this is Class D talking about her in a room she is not in. Chapter 4 gives her no line of her own until her own section later in the chapter; nothing here is her choice. p.77–82

Ch.4 §4.9–4.10: she lets two people take a camera to a repair counterp.132–142
22 entries · 13 quoted
  • Scene

    She has already arrived and is unnoticed at her own meeting: "Had Sakura really been sitting there the entire time? Amazing that we hadn’t noticed her at all. Not a sign of her presence or aura." p.136

  • Spoken

    "I’m sorry, I don’t stand out much, I suppose… Good morning," — the first line she speaks aloud in the volume, and she apologises for it before it is needed. p.136

  • Spoken

    "Oh, you don’t need to say that for my sake, Ayanokouji-kun." — his compliment that he felt her presence is declined. p.136

  • Feat

    Her chosen disguise, as read by the boy who has never seen her before: "Sakura was wearing a hat, and even a surgical face mask, which made it difficult to recognize her at a glance." and "She didn’t seem to have a cold. Rather, she seemed like the type to wear the mask in order to avoid attention. She must have really hated standing out." p.136

  • Spoken

    "Yeah, I suppose so. I think I do stand out, especially here," — and the mask comes off: "Sheepishly, she removed her mask." p.136

  • Spoken

    "So anyway, about my digital camera. Is it okay if we go to the electronics store in the mall?" — the ask that opens the day, and it is hers. p.136

  • Spoken

    "I wonder if they’ll fix it right away…" — "Sakura, looked rather anxious as she took out her digital camera and held it tight." p.138

  • Spoken

    "Yeah. It’s weird, isn’t it?" — on being asked whether she loves the camera. p.138

  • Limit

    The repair counter stops her cold in front of two classmates: "For some reason, Sakura suddenly stopped in her tracks. When I glanced over at her, I noticed she was wearing an expression of blatant fear and revulsion." p.138

  • Spoken

    "Ah, umm… Well…" and then "It’s nothing." — "She donned an earnest smile, and walked to the repair counter." p.139

  • Feat

    Kushida takes the entire transaction: "Sakura, the camera’s owner, spoke only when she needed to give her consent or to clarify something." p.139

  • Scene

    What the camera actually is, technically: "when the store clerk opened up the camera to confirm the contents, he saw that part of it had been damaged from the fall. That was why the camera wouldn’t turn on properly. Fortunately, because Sakura still had the warranty card, the item could be repaired free of charge." — the contents are never described. p.139

  • Feat

    The cost of having her address written down by someone else: "Sakura gently patted her chest and sighed in apparent relief, but when she learned it’d take two weeks for the camera to be repaired, she grew discouraged. Her shoulders slumped." p.140

  • Spoken

    "Didn’t you feel kind of disgusted?" — to Kushida, about the clerk; the first time she questions someone else's reading of a room. p.141

  • Spoken

    "That was kind of what I was trying to get at before… I was scared to go to the repair shop alone because of that…" p.141

  • Scene

    She turns to the quietest of the three for a verdict on a man who frightened her: "Turning to me, she asked" and then "What do you think, Ayanokouji-kun?" p.141

  • Spoken

    "Th-thank you…Ayanokouji-kun. You really…saved me." p.141

  • Spoken

    "Since you were with me today, Kushida-san, we completed our task without me having to talk at all. Thank you." p.141

  • Spoken

    The camera's history, given unprompted: "Yeah… I’ve liked cameras ever since I was little. My dad bought me one before I entered junior high, and I absolutely fell in love with it. Or I suppose you could say that I just love taking pictures… I’m not really wellinformed when it comes to this stuff, though." — the missing hyphen is the translation's. p.142

  • Limit

    The one question that makes her physically retreat: "Sakura stepped backwards, looking quite flustered." then "Sakura shut her mouth, and her body stiffened." and "Th-that’s a secret." p.142

  • Spoken

    "W-well, it’s just… It’s embarrassing," — "replied Sakura, her cheeks reddening. She looked down as she spoke." p.142

  • Caveat

    Whether she photographs people is asked but not answered in this volume, and the asker is not tagged in the translation; only her refusal is hers. p.142

  • Spoken

    "Umm… Thank you for everything today. You really helped me out," p.144

  • Spoken

    "Ah… O-okay… I…I got it. I’ll do my best." p.145

  • Spoken

    "I-It’s okay. I…will." — and the delivery of even that: "Sakura kept her eyes down as she spoke. Partway through her sentence she began to sputter, and her words faded so much that we couldn’t hear them. However, it didn’t seem as though she felt uncomfortable." p.145

  • Feat

    She accepts a change in how she talks rather than lose the two people who came with her: "I thought that Sakura would have rejected the notion, but she managed to squeak out a few words of agreement." p.145

  • Spoken

    "Well!" — "Sakura spoke in a small voice, but looked straight at us. When our gazes met, however, she immediately averted her eyes." p.146

  • Spoken

  • Spoken

    "Yes. I saw everything. It was a total coincidence, though… I’m sure you don’t believe me." p.146

  • Spoken

    Her stated reason for three weeks of silence: "It’s okay… I think that if I kept quiet, I’d probably come to regret it. I…don’t want to cause trouble for my classmates. But, if I spoke up as a witness, then…I would definitely stand out. I hated the thought of that… I’m sorry." p.146

  • Feat

    She apologises while giving the class exactly what it has been asking for; "While Sakura apologized to us repeatedly, full of remorse, she also promised Kushida that she would testify." p.146

  • Trait

    Everything the case needed was available from her on the first day; what changed is not her courage but her company — she testifies the moment she has two people standing beside her instead of a crowd asking her alone. p.132–146

  • Relationship

    She tells Kushida the camera history, refuses Kushida the glasses, and hands Kushida the testimony — the friendship is real before the evidence is. — Kushida Kikyō p.142, p.144–146

  • Relationship

    He wrote his own name and dorm room on her repair form so she would not have to hand over her address; her first unscheduled call is to him, not to Kushida. — Ayanokōji Kiyotaka p.140

Ch.3 §3.1: the class's own memory of him is the case against himp.42–45
10 entries · 5 quoted
  • Scene

    Classmates answer Kushida's appeal with their own sightings, speakers unnamed: "I saw him grab some kid by the collar just because they bumped into each other in the hallway." / "I saw him cut in line in the cafeteria and lash out when someone tried to tell him that wasn’t okay." p.42–43

  • Backstory

    Yamauchi's public counter-testimony, which turns the room: that Sudou "lied so he could justify what he did", that "He talked about beating up kids all the time in junior high. He even told us how much fun it was to beat people up." p.42

  • Scene

    In the cafeteria he pleads to Horikita in flat, external terms: "Sudou spoke to Horikita with indifference, almost as if he were describing someone else’s problems." p.44

  • Spoken

  • Spoken

  • Spoken

    “What the hell is her deal?! Goddamn it!” p.45

  • Feat

    His temper performs the argument against him in real time: "Unable to direct his rage anywhere else, Sudou slammed his fists on the cafeteria table, spilling a nearby student’s miso soup. The student glared, but when he saw how scary Sudou looked, he remained silent." p.45

  • Relationship

    He assumes the friendship she refused to name; her answer — that she has "never once considered you a friend" — is the first time the class hears her draw that line out loud. — Horikita Suzune p.44–45

  • Spoken

  • Scene

  • Trait
